## 2026-10-26 — Result rows link to series pages minutes after a map

Branch `result-links`, base `origin/main`. Worker uploads maps, results and final pages before dispatch; unsettled snapshots preserve settlement's debounce. Prices restores archives, overlays local links and stages live-results HTML with Predictions/Results; missing/broken archives retain fetched links. Figures embed SVG. Local filesystem-backed S3 shim: `live_archive.sh` 0.770 s new map, 0.093 s cached (one map, cached series record). Warm render medians, five runs: absent 0.0037 s, restored 0.0057 s (+0.0020 s). Added cost enables prompt links without full builds. AWS/API latency and Actions timing unmeasured. Copy review: PASS (no copy changed).

## 2026-10-26 — Shared capital, series cap, position-aware live maps, idle-only restarts

Removed the map budget; pregame and live maps share free capital. Optional global `series_fraction` caps combined held cost; live targets subtract same-team active filled contracts across strategies. Existing map-only caps and legacy budget releases remain. Refresh flags wait until no tracked game is live; worker exit 5 requests systemd restart. Root `deploy/live/install.sh` must run once for the unit change. Added [sizing evidence](../research/pregame_sizing.md) and its CSV-driven script. Checks: ruff format/check; full pytest (1,082 passed, 2 skipped); feed/steering checks (21 passed); research script --help; bash -n; diff --check. Regression checks add no CI job/build step; Actions timing unmeasured. Limit: the global series cap is not enforced atomically across concurrent pregame and map reservations. No SSM values changed; 10% awaits configuration.

## 2026-10-26 — Live model: fixed FORGE offset tested, rejected

Branch `live-offset`, base `1a74d73`. `scripts/research/live_offset.py` ([report](../research/live_offset.md)); viewed test split. D enters logit(FORGE) as an offset (calls start at the pre-map call), fits its fade, drops Elo and calibration: −0.0024 (−0.0062 to +0.0011), 0–20 min better, 30+ worse (+0.0072): FAIL. Diagnosis: production refit on D's maps loses the same at 30+; Elo, calibration and prior source don't matter, the 1,237 dropped maps without FORGE do. F (all maps, Elo prior where FORGE is missing): −0.0008 (−0.0035 to +0.0019), ECE 0.0194 → 0.0086, 30+ still worse (+0.0030, +0.0006 to +0.0054): FAIL; FORGE maps −0.0037 (significant). No runtime change. Checks: ruff, full pytest.

## 2026-10-26 — Live worker: feed back-off, game retries, start tolerance, CloudWatch logs

Audit (Oct 23–26): 13 tracked maps, 2,059 decisions; 1,153 pause-bug skips fixed earlier in #124. GEN v LYON began 20:59Z against Riot's 22:00Z start; ten matches went untracked; HLE v IG dropped on HTTP 404. Shared two-hour start tolerance preserves earlier-day ratings. HTTP 400 doubles per-game lag (30–600s); 30 successes halve it. Other window errors retry for 30 polls; failed IDs expire after 300s. Decisions carry match/game/map; worker events persist except cycles. Rerunnable install configures CloudWatch collection for both logs. Checks: ruff; pytest 1,060 passed, 2 skipped (10.01s; existing cases 11.80s); 13 recovery tests 0.30s; bash -n; diff --check. Actions timing unmeasured. Limit: Riot's longer delay on Oct 24 is inferred, not observed; agent and log groups need the infrastructure PR and an install.sh rerun.

## 2026-10-26 — Live maps: pregame positions, pause check, executor log

BLG v KC 18:20Z: KC (18¢ ask, 30.7% blended) skipped with `existing_fixture_exposure`; pregame KC held the same Bo1 tickers (PR #123 fixed only ledger fences). That exchange check is now pregame-only; positions still count toward exposure. The worker's `flat` compared the last two frames (0.17 s apart), rejecting 81% of BLG v KC polls as paused; it now needs ten seconds of unchanged gold after two minutes, as `feed_timelines.py` defines it for the benchmark. The live handler prints one JSON line per signal (outcome, reason) to CloudWatch. New end-to-end test (outbox → SQS pointer → handler → real Kalshi over faked HTTP → ledger with a pregame position) failed before the fix. Checks: ruff; pause and log tests; full pytest 1,044 passed, 2 skipped. Limit: one candidate per map; worker needs a restart.

## 2026-10-26 — Live maps no longer blocked by pregame fences

Both live candidates so far (DK v G2 10-23, DK at 25¢ vs 34% model; G2 v TES 10-25, TES at 42¢ vs 52%) were rejected: same-side pregame orders held their Bo1 event fences, and pregame held ($4.09, $2.48) exceeded the shared ~$0.93 2% series cap. It would also have blocked Bo3/Bo5 maps. Maps now use MAPEVENT#/MAPSERIES# (shadow sizing too); pregame unchanged apart from storing series_key; old intents release against SERIES#. Checks: ruff; tests for the Bo1 collision, map fence, MAPSERIES# cap and release, legacy release; full pytest (1,037 passed, 2 skipped). Limit: correlated exposure (the live prior reuses the pregame rating) is bounded only by map/series/budget caps; executor redeploys on merge.

## 2026-10-23 — Live feed polls on the window boundary; recorded-page guard

Feed windows start on 10 s boundaries 30 s back, so the newest frame's age is ~20.6 s plus the poll's offset into the cycle (BLG v MKOI: 20.6 s at +0 s rising to 29.8 s at +9 s; median 25.6 s). The worker now polls once per 10 s slot, 0.5 s after it opens (`feed_slot`), and wakes for it: ~5 s fresher on average, no extra requests (`--interval` must be 10, 20 or 30). After a restart the old code wrote BLG v MKOI's waiting page over its recorded map page (restored from `map1.json`); `without_recorded` now skips waiting pages for matches whose file holds frames. Checks: new timing and guard tests; cadence test on slot-aligned clock; full pytest; ruff. Limit: alignment unmeasured live.

## 2026-10-23 — Live-map delay and fight-veto research; markout script

Research only (`docs/research/live_latency.md`). Production newest-frame age is 22–26 s (median, two maps; ~20 s Riot floor); the replay assumes 21 s. `evaluate_live.py --build-data --lag N --data PATH` builds at another delay. Lag 26 vs 21 (1,772 maps, 30 s rule): 171 vs 183 bets, +8.6% vs +21.3% ROI; paired −1.4¢/map (−3.5 to +0.6), includes zero. Fixed fight/objective veto: −2.0¢/map (−4.0 to −0.2); the 71 bets it removed had +48.7% ROI and +3.9¢ CLV, so no veto. New `scripts/live_markouts.py` reads backed-up candidates (30/60/120 s quote markouts). Checks: ruff; full pytest; both builds and the report run locally. No production, CI or pin change. Limits: reused history, minute samples, tape costs.

Limits: needs infra PR #22 (1-minute schedule, token access); buying up to start was unproven in the timing backtest (PR #111); untested against live Kalshi/GitHub.

## 2026-10-19 — More evidence for Elo league offsets

[Research](../research/elo_offsets.md) selected through 2021: team-only non-international cross-league links .5, compensated transfers .02/20 games; now replay defaults. Held-out cross-league log loss −.012435 (series CI −.016476 to −.008520), others within noise; level-bias RMS .430785→.317342. Calibration removal inconclusive; move-down bonus still needed. Refitted six forecast coefficients (≤1.6% movement), 12 Form coefficients and 95 league slopes; exact deltas in report. Rebuilt DB; regenerated forecast/season reports; offline site build and Elo/FORGE HTML/JSON spot-check passed. Checks: pytest 968 passed, 2 skipped; ruff format/check. New default test <.005s locally; Actions cost unmeasured. Limits: market and native guards judged by PR check; calibration frozen on old offsets; historical holdouts previously viewed; out-of-year refits aren’t chronological deployment.

## 2026-10-19 — Elo's forecast curves can't be removed (step 3 of the review)

Research only. `elo_one_scale.py` ([report](../research/elo_one_scale.md)): one update slope with tier K (training picks M 24 / O 20, share 1) is no better held out (other leagues +.0005, significant) and its no-curve forecast fails non-inferiority in 4/4 Elo sections (+.006 to +.015). Elo's expected score is compressed about 1.5× in every tier; raising K lowers that but loses accuracy. Cause (`elo_score_compression.py`): the game-length score; with 1/0 results c ≈ 1 but training loss +.005. The exploratory win-scale update (`elo_win_scale.py`, [report](../research/elo_win_scale.md)) is calibrated near K 24 but worse: cross-league +.016, other leagues +.004; no curve +.011 to +.021. Verdict: keep the fitted curves; regional effects are an offset level bias. Checks: published replay matches DB (9e−13); ruff; steering tests.

## 2026-10-19 — Fresh calls, part 1: resume Elo with Leaguepedia results hourly

`EloState` makes the replay resumable (rebuilt Elo tables identical row for row). Publish saves the state (3.1 MB, ~11 s); the hourly job's new step replays Leaguepedia games Oracle's Elixir lacks (each matched to an OE game of the same pair within 3 hours, or applied) and refreshes unstarted calls; Form stays frozen, `data_through` stays the OE date. ## 2026-10-19 — Call staleness measured

`scripts/research/call_staleness.py` on the published prediction log: of 74 settled calls frozen at the start (since Oct 4), 40 used data one day old and 34 two to four days old (Oracle's Elixir lag). Rebuilding 4,201 settled logged series with older ratings than the backtest's day-before: one more day +0.0039 series log loss (+0.0007 to +0.0074), two +0.0053, seven +0.0134; international (155) within noise. So the backtest and market report flatter published calls, and fresher results (Leaguepedia before Oracle's Elixir) are worth about .004 a day of lag. Same-day results are not measured. Research only; no published change.

## 2026-10-19 — Series-clustered forecast intervals, forward holdout, addition/removal rules

Forecast report/dumps and PR comparisons now resample whole series; dumps carry series/ISO dates, with head metadata supporting older bases. Forward rows from 2026-10-20 are unguarded. Rules require ≥0.0005 log-loss gain for additions and upper CI <+0.0005 for removals. Clustering barely widens intervals (~2 games a series; domestic FORGE vs Elo −0.0028 to −0.0009 both ways), so game-level intervals were not the problem. Report runtime: 37.80 s before, 28.33 s after (local, no CI work added). Checks: report, unchanged `--check-weights`, dump/self-comparison (exit 0), pytest (902 passed, 2 skipped), ruff format/check, diff whitespace. Limit: research scripts in `scripts/research` retain game-level intervals.

## 2026-10-19 — Live benchmark refresh: first CI runs, seed fix

First dispatch of `live-bench.yml` failed: the hand seed was made with macOS `tar`, whose AppleDouble `._*.json` files broke JSON reads on Linux. Reseeded with `COPYFILE_DISABLE=1 tar --no-xattrs` (checked with Python `tarfile`: no metadata entries) and fixed the seed commands in `deployment.md`. Second run (39327787116) passed in 10 min: Riot's feed served the GitHub runner without refusals (14 new maps, 81 s), `--build-data` took 7.9 min (CPU-bound; it re-reduces every map, so it grows with the data), state synced to S3, and `live-bench-data-63ee7c2a…` (2.2 MB) cached: 1,772 maps / 823 series, up from 1,666 / 813. Limit: no PR has read the cached entry yet.

## 2026-10-19 — Weekly live benchmark data refresh in CI

New `live-bench.yml` (Mondays, main only, read-only contents; commits nothing): restores the feed timelines and Kalshi tapes from `s3://…/prometheus/live-bench/`, collects new feed maps in four shards, runs `--build-data --refresh-markets`, syncs grown state back and saves `live-bench-data-<hash>` to the Actions cache. `benchmarks.yml` restores that one entry for both sides (in the markets dump key); the release pin stays as fallback. `feed_timelines.py schedule` stops paging at saved matches. ## 2026-10-17 — Series score in live calls, feed timeline backfill and model v2 refit

Branch `series-checkpoint` from `eb11b9e` (PR #66).

- **Series shift (live service):** `series.map_shift`/`map_prior`; `Game.score_before` (the score before this map; none while a counted map's winner is unknown); `timeline_calls(..., shift)` moves the pre-map and every in-game call; the FORGE pre-map call uses `map_prior`. The checkpoint report now computes its shift with `map_shift` and reproduces exactly (diffed). Tests: map shift, score before, shifted calls (`tests/live`, 63 passed).
- **`scripts/research/feed_timelines.py`** (+ `feed_timelines_collect.sh`): 9,046 completed maps since 2024 in 16 leagues listed (`schedule.json`, 25 min); collection at one window a minute (~32 requests, 17 s a map), four shards. First run stopped at 91 maps when this laptop slept (connection timeouts, exit 3 then); now "no answer" exits 4 and the runner reruns a minute later, refusals still stop. Later one shard stopped on a single 403 (the same request returned 200 shortly after; restarted by hand) and one crashed on a connection dropped mid-read (`IncompleteRead`, now counted as no answer); the runner takes shard indices to restart only those. Two more isolated 403s by 21:30 (each URL returned 200 on one later try), so the runner now waits 5 minutes after a refusal and reruns once; a shard refused again before saving another map stops for good. Shards finish unevenly, so `--split j/m` shares one shard's still-missing maps among m runs (the runner passes `EXTRA` arguments); shard 1's last ~120 maps ran on four. Found: `getEventDetails` sides are often swapped against the feed's metadata and Oracle's Elixir (checked on two 2024 maps), so the join ignores them and the collector records the feed's blue team; some maps have no feed data (204: 10 of the first 159, 6 LPL); the feed marks pauses (`paused`, one frozen frame, one pause 17 minutes).
- **`scripts/research/train_time_model_v2.py`**: protocol fixed before scoring (see the script). Dry run on 155 maps: 140 joined; estimated clock vs Oracle's Elixir game length within −114 to +40 s (1st–99th percentile) after removing marked pauses (before: up to +998 s). Full run (2026-10-17, all 9,046 maps; 8,347 joined; clock vs game length median −5 s, 90% within −52 to +1 s): train 3,533 maps, calibrate 1,733, test 3,857. **v2 not adopted**: v2 − v1 +0.0002 (−0.0011 to +0.0016); better only after 30 minutes (−0.0053, −0.0092 to −0.0013) and outside major leagues (−0.0027). v1 stays. With 3× the maps the same six features don't improve, while a per-checkpoint gold+Elo refit did beat v1 at 15–20 minutes (series checkpoint report): the limit looks like the model's time terms, not data. **Series shift on the feed test** (2,406 maps 2+, v1): −0.0002 (−0.0029 to +0.0025); minutes 0–10 −0.0018 (−0.0053 to +0.0016); domestic majors +0.0009 (−0.0019 to +0.0036). It does not confirm the checkpoint gain, nor show harm; kept live, unconfirmed. Report `docs/research/time_model_v2.md`; model and rows in `data/research/time_model_v2/` (not shipped).

- **`scripts/research/series_checkpoint.py`** (new; report `docs/research/series_checkpoint.md`, tests `tests/research/test_series_checkpoint.py`): the live map model's call (Elo from before the series, as the live page uses it) at Oracle's Elixir's 0/10/15/20/25-minute gold, with and without the series lead, on maps 2+ of clean Bo3/Bo5 (fit 2018–2024, scored 2025–2026, series-resampled intervals). Major teams: the series model's Beta shift cuts log loss 0.0039 (0 min), 0.0038 (10), 0.0028 (15), 0.0019 (20, interval includes 0). About half the lead is Elo moving between maps (with per-map Elo the major-team gain is under 0.0015, intervals include 0). A gold + Elo checkpoint refit beats the live model at 15 and 20 minutes (major −0.0041/−0.0043; other −0.0063/−0.0073).
- **`series_independence.load`**: removed its home-league merge, which collided with the `home`/`opp_home` columns `gamelog.add_calls` now adds (the script failed on current main).
- **Riot feed check:** `feed.lolesports.com/livestats/v1/window/<game>?startingTime=` returned frames for a 2026-05 LCK game and a 2024-01 LCK game (the oldest LCK schedule page); `getSchedule` pages back to January 2024.
- Added future work: past series kept on the live pages with their graphs; the series term and more training timelines for the live model.
- Checks: `pytest tests/live tests/research tests/test_steering.py`; `ruff check` and `ruff format` on the new files; report regenerated from the script. Live model's GOL training maps (2022–2023) are a small part of the fit years and none of the scored years. Not checked: older feed coverage before 2024, request rate limits for a bulk backfill.

## 2026-10-17 — Freeze the draft model; Kalshi's book before each map

Branch `draft-frozen`, base `main` at `761b1a3` (PR #78). User asked to merge #78 (done), freeze the draft model, record Kalshi's book around the draft, and look for a pick source; and asked whether trading in the first 30 s of the game would do instead of champion select.

- **Trading after the start** (3,146 priced maps, Kalshi's last trade at each offset, full draft shift): weight beside Kalshi +0.87 (+0.53 to +1.22) at −60 s, +0.82 at the start, +0.76 (+0.42 to +1.10) at +30 s, +0.71 (+0.36 to +1.04) at +60 s, +0.67 at +3 min; Kalshi's move from −60 s correlates 0.06–0.15 with the shift. The feed's picks (first frame with champions ~32 s after the start on the pilot) keep roughly 80% of the draft signal, so no champion-select source is required.
- **Pick source:** the public lolesports endpoints the worker uses (getLive, getEventDetails, the livestats window) carry no picks before the game loads (pilot: window 204 for 16 minutes, picks with the first frame). Nothing was live in a draft to probe further. Champion select would need reading the stream; recorded as optional.
- **Frozen draft model:** `draft_score.py --freeze` wrote `scripts/research/draft_model_v1.json` (base and draft + matchup + synergy weights, constants, fit 2019-01-01 to 2026-10-11 on 76,299 games, script hash); it refuses to overwrite. `--prospective` scores only later games, against Elo and against Kalshi at 60 s before and 45 s after the start with paper bets on Kalshi's price plus the frozen shift; nothing after 2026-10-11 has been looked at. Smoke-tested on a copy dated 2026-09-27 (in-sample, code check only).
- **Live worker, book before the map:** `service.PreMaps` reads Kalshi's map book every cycle while getLive lists the map in progress and the feed has no frames (draft, loading), into `<match>/<game>/pre_quotes.jsonl` with the top five levels a side (`kalshi.book_levels`), logs `pre_map_seen`, and stops once the map is tracked; in-game quote rows also carry the five levels. `backup.sh` syncs `pre_quotes.jsonl`. Not yet run on a real match.
- **Lost and refetched data:** removing the #78 worktree with `--force` deleted its 8,096 pre-map trade tapes (gitignored). They are being refetched into the main checkout's `data/live_bench/pre_trades/`, and AGENTS.md now shares both tape caches by symlink and warns to move fetched data before removing a worktree.
- **Data and results:** refetch done (3,286 whole-map tapes, 6,141 pre-map tapes in the main checkout). Rebuilt with the grown timelines: 1,590 feed maps / 805 series, 3,346 pre-maps; published `live_maps-352ddac1b30f.json.gz` (1.6 MB), fresh fetch verified, dump 2.1 s. With 3x the maps our in-game call's weight beside Kalshi is now significant (+0.30, +0.17 to +0.43; was +0.21 on 406 maps). In-game paper bets looked very profitable (quarter Kelly ×269 at a 5-point edge) but it is a stale-price artifact: keeping only calls whose latest trade is under 20 s old and pricing a side no lower than that trade, returns drop to within noise (+2% to +4%) except one setting (+12.7%, CLV 0); a quiet market on a trade tape is a stale price. Report Limits now says so; the recorded book will price them.
- **Rebased on PR #79** (Kalshi quotes every 2 s): the loop now also runs at that cadence while a map is only in its draft (`pre.maps`), so the draft's book is read every 2 s; conflicts in `current_state.md` and `tests/live/test_live_service.py` kept both sides. The loop-cadence condition has no test of its own.
- **Held-price rule** (user: bet only after Kalshi's price stays the same for a while, not once quiet): `first_bets(calls, edge, window, tol)` bets only when at least 2 trades fall in the window before our call arrives, all within the tolerance, priced from a trade backing our side in that window and never below the latest price; the quiet rule is removed. Seven new per-sample fields per window (range, count, last cost a side for 10/21/30 s). On 1,666 feed maps at a 5-point edge: no setting is above zero significantly; exactly flat for 21 s −14.2% (−23.6% to −4.9%), for 30 s −15.7% (−25.8% to −5.3%); ±1¢ −5.4% to +1.2%, within noise; ±2¢ +10% to +11% with wide intervals and long-shot driven. Data rebuilt in 3.5 min from cached tapes and published as `live_maps-0705f02b1edf.json.gz` (1,666 feed maps, 2.2 MB); fresh fetch verified.
- `draft_score.py --prospective`: 0 games after 2026-10-11 in the DB yet (Oracle's Elixir lags); report `docs/research/draft_prospective.md`.
- Checks: `uv run pytest -q` 722 passed; ruff clean. Limits: one pilot for the feed's pick timing; the pre-map book poller is tested with a stub, not live.

- **Kalshi through the draft** (213 feed maps with prices at every mark): trades rise from 14 to 55 in the 12 minutes before the start, but the price moves little (mean 2.8 points, like 2.4 before) and Kalshi's log loss a minute before is no better than 12 minutes before (+0.0035, −0.0113 to +0.0210).
- **`scripts/research/draft_score.py`** (report `docs/research/draft_score.md`): protocol fixed before scoring (see the script). Held-out 2025–26: draft −0.0027 (−0.0039 to −0.0014) on 18,718 games; per feature: champion-role recent results −0.0022, experience −0.0004, player results and lane gold on the champion ≈ 0. Exploratory second round: lane matchups −0.0008, team champion pairs −0.0014, both −0.0016 (−0.0023 to −0.0009) on top. Against Kalshi a minute before the start (3,159 maps): shift weight +0.90 (+0.55 to +1.25); Kalshi's move through the draft uncorrelated with the shift (−0.00); paper bets on Kalshi + shift up to +6.8% flat ROI (−0.0% to +13.5%) at a 3-point edge. A first pass on only the 163 feed-timeline maps had pointed the other way (draft worse than Elo there, +0.018); the full priced set reverses it.
- **Live benchmark pre-map section** (`evaluate_live.py`): `build_premaps` matches every settled map market to its OE game and stores Kalshi 12 minutes and a minute before the start; `replay_premaps` scores the live page's pregame call; report section 5 and a guarded "pre-map, every priced map" band in `compare_benchmarks.py` (kept out of "all"). Result: the live model's pregame call trails Kalshi by +0.019 (+0.007 to +0.031) on 3,330 maps and is overconfident (quarter Kelly loses ~97%); a plain Elo logistic does better (0.6229 vs 0.6365 on the draft script's priced maps).
- **Cache fix:** pre-map tapes (half an hour before the start) live in `data/live_bench/pre_trades/`, apart from whole-map tapes, so a short tape is never read as a whole map's; this run's 8,096 short tapes were moved there before any build.
- **PR check fix:** the first run of this PR crashed in `compare_live` (base dump's game ids numeric, head's mixed with OE text ids) and the crash's exit code 1 read as "significantly worse". Keys are now read as text, and `compare_benchmarks.py` exits 2 on any exception, which the workflow already reports as an error, not a failed benchmark. Tests for both.
- **Speed:** `EloBefore` indexes each team's Elo once; the dump with pre-maps went from 12.0 s to 1.3 s locally, byte-identical output.
- **Data:** rebuilt with the grown timelines (563 feed maps, 442 series; 3,332 pre-maps) and published as `live_maps-77df40cd8691.json.gz` (0.7 MB) under the existing `live-bench-data` release (user's standing approval); a fresh `--fetch-data` passed the hash check.
- Checks: `uv run pytest -q` 719 passed (new tests: OE match by map number and result, pre-map prices and bets, pre-map replay and dump band, the guarded band kept out of "all"); ruff clean; dump unchanged by the Elo index. Limits: OE's game time stands for the start and the draft's timing is estimated; Kalshi's side is last trades, not the ask; the second-round draft features were scored on viewed test years; no live pick source yet.

FURIA map 4 gains 25 minute quotes and a pre-map 45; KC v MVK map 2 gains its pre-map 83.5 (its line was recorded live). Compact copies carry `schema` 2; `live_archive.sh` remakes older ones (all three were remade and uploaded).

Pointer or arrow keys (Shift: 5 minutes) read any moment as on the watch page: ledger-blue rule and dot, a framed reading beside it (time, Ours, Kalshi, gold lead) and a hidden live region.

- **Before each map.** The series page's Table I lists every played map (recorded or not): the score before it, our map and series chances and Kalshi's for both in the minute before it, and the winner (hidden on phones). `archive.series_record` (run by `live_archive.sh` once a match is over, saved as `<match>/series.min.json`; schema 1) takes every map's game from Riot's `getEventDetails`, its start from the feed's first frame (unrecorded maps included), its winner from Kalshi's settled map market, and Kalshi's map and series prices from the contracts' minute candlesticks. Our pre-map chance (table and the figure's open circle) now comes from the logged call before the series, flipped to the page's order, and the service's file only when the log lacks the match. Two small pages and one small script per series; no other job changes.

## 2026-10-16 — Live benchmark in the PR check

Branch `live-market-bench`, rebased on `main` at `5806c9a` (PR #75). The user asked for the replay to become a CI benchmark guarding the live model, chose a GitHub release asset for its data and a log-loss guard by minute band.

- **`scripts/evaluate_live.py`** replaces `scripts/research/live_markets_retro.py` (removed, with its test and report). It replays calls through the live service's own `timeline.timeline_calls` and model path (new `timeline.MODEL_PATH`, now the service's `--model` default), so a PR that changes the model or the call is scored as the service would publish it. `--build-data` reduces timelines and trade tapes to about 1 KB a map (sides from the feed's new `blue_id`, else OE; series score before each map stored for the coming series shift); `--publish-data` / `--fetch-data` move it through the `live-bench-data` release, pinned by hash in `scripts/live_bench_data.json`. Report `docs/live_report.md`.
- **`compare_benchmarks.py`** gains a Live section: log loss head − base per band (pregame, 5–10, 15–20, 25–30, all), series resampled, all guarded. `benchmarks.yml` runs `evaluate_live.py --fetch-data --dump dumps/live` in the market job after the market benchmark; a side without the pin file skips it.
- Data now: 406 maps / 336 series (686 feed games since 2025-10-27 in the timelines collected so far; 257 without a market, 9 winner mismatches, 14 without in-game trades; feed `blue_id` and OE disagree on 3). Kalshi still ahead: pooled 5–30 min +0.0245 (+0.0039 to +0.0459); our weight +0.21 (−0.01 to +0.44); paper bets with any quiet window and edge within noise.
- **Held-out rule:** each live dump records its model's fit date (`fit_through` in the model JSON, else the end of its last training or calibration year; v1: 2024-12-31) and the comparison scores only maps after the later of base's and head's, so a refit on 2026 maps is never judged on maps it was fit on. A refit should set `fit_through`. Tested with a head that is worse only on maps before its fit date (fails without the rule, passes with it).
- The order book around each live call is already saved by the worker (every 10 s, raw in each map's `archive.tar.gz`, backed up); the replay uses trades because past maps have no book.
- Gate check: replaying with the model's Elo terms zeroed makes every band significantly worse (all +0.013 to +0.052, intervals above 0); identical dumps pass.
- Cost: dump 1.9 s wall locally (replay 1.1 s), report 7.5 s; the data download is under 1 MB. Runs in the market job after the market benchmark (adds a few seconds to that job, nothing to the core job or publish build).
- Checks: `uv run pytest -q tests/test_evaluate_live.py tests/test_compare_benchmarks.py` (8 + 16 pass); full suite and ruff below. With the user's approval, created the public `live-bench-data` release and uploaded `live_maps-ede352b887b1.json.gz` (406 maps), pinned in `scripts/live_bench_data.json`; a fresh `--fetch-data` from the release passed the hash check and replayed 405 maps. First Actions run (PR #77): every check passed; the head's Live benchmark step took 4 s (download and replay) after the 3 min 39 s market benchmark in the same job; the base has no pin, so the Live section read "missing from one side".

## 2026-10-16 — Replay in-game calls against Kalshi's in-game trades

Branch `live-market-bench`, base `main` at `e502358`. Research only: new `scripts/research/live_markets_retro.py` (report `docs/research/live_markets_retro.md`, tests `tests/research/test_live_markets_retro.py`) replays the live in-game model (`time_model_v1.json`) over saved Riot feed timelines (`data/feed_timelines/games/`, about one sample a minute) for every map with a settled `KXLOLMAP` market, and reads Kalshi's per-trade tape (`/historical/trades` before Kalshi's cutoff, `/markets/trades` after; cached in `data/research/live_markets/trades/`). The user asked for numbers 1, 2, 4 and 5 (log loss by minute, our weight beside the market, paper bets placed only after Kalshi has been quiet, gap/trading/spread), measured lag and quarter-Kelly sizing.

Feed lag measured from the KC–C9 pilot's request log (`../lol-live/data/live/115660588583171786/`): the newest frame received was never under 20 s old, median 21.1 s on map 5 (steady polling), 26.1 s on map 4. Lag 21 s is the default; 0/10/30 reported.

Data fix found on the way: on 100 of the matched maps the saved feed timelines attach the team names to the wrong side (their blue/red stats are right). KOI–G2 2026-02-23 map 1 is labelled KOI blue; OE has G2 blue, and blue won 10 towers to 4. Sides now come from OE's game between the two teams nearest the feed start. Winner mismatches against Kalshi's result fell from 100 to 7 (dropped). The live service itself checks each side's team ID against the window metadata (`feed.py`), so it would reject such frames, not mis-assign them.

Results (358 maps, 304 series, 2025-10-27 to 2026-10, series bootstrap): Kalshi's last trade beats our call at every minute; ours − Kalshi log loss +0.046 (+0.011 to +0.081) at 15 min and +0.049 (+0.018 to +0.082) at 20 min, minutes 5–30 pooled +0.032 (+0.009 to +0.055), about the same at lag 0 as at 21 s. Our weight beside Kalshi's log-odds, minutes 5–30: +0.15 (−0.07 to +0.41), not significant. Paper bets (first per map, edge ≥ 5 pts, lag 21 s): quiet 10 s 312 bets, flat ROI +5.5% (−9.3% to +22.5%), quarter-Kelly growth +0.0012/bet (−0.0057 to +0.0086), CLV −0.2 pts; quiet 21 s −2.9% (−16.0% to +11.0%). No setting has an interval excluding zero. Markets trade about 10 times a minute in game; about half of calls arrive after ≥10 s without a trade; implied spread 3–4 cents.

Checks: `uv run pytest -q tests/research/test_live_markets_retro.py` (8 pass); full suite and ruff below. First run fetched ~760 trade tapes in about 3 minutes; reruns from cache take about 20 s. No CI, site or metric change. Limits: last trade stands for the book (quiet = no trade); calls once a minute instead of every few seconds; one pilot match for the lag; 2026 was development evidence for the in-game model, so this is retrospective.

## 2026-10-16 — Live: Kalshi names through aliases, map sides from the feed

Branch `live-kalshi-aliases`, base `42b97f3` (PR #70). Two production faults on Worlds Play-Ins day:

- **No Kalshi odds on FURIA v C9.** Kalshi calls the team "FURIA Esports"; `live/kalshi.contract` matched only our, Riot's and the code's names exactly, so neither the map nor the series contract was found. Names in the rules text now also go through `markets.MARKET_ALIASES` (already has FURIA Esports → FURIA). Against the open markets saved during map 4: before, none; after, `KXLOLMAP-26OCT161900C9FUR-4-FUR` and `KXLOLGAME-26OCT161900C9FUR-FUR`.
- **Only map 4 of FURIA v C9 (and maps 1–2 of KC v MVK) on Live.** The service took each map's blue/red teams from getLive, which at a map's start can still list the previous map's sides (Riot corrected them later: getEventDetails now has the right ones). The collector then refuses every frame (`blue/red_team_identity_mismatch`, shown on KC v MVK map 3's last page), the map never finishes and no snapshot is written; the later snapshot attempt also fails because the re-selection's sides differ from the saved session's. Side swaps today: KC v MVK map 3, FURIA v C9 maps 2 and 3 (FURIA map 4 kept map 3's sides and worked). The worker's collectors confirm it for FURIA v C9 maps 1–3: each saved selection has blue and red reversed from the feed (map 1 was selected 11 minutes before its start with C9 blue; FURIA was blue), each stuck at `no_usable_current_observation: blue/red_team_identity_mismatch`, and `snapshot_failed: session ID already used` repeated every minute for all three until the match left getLive. The journal also showed getLive relisting map 1 as in progress at 21:40, after map 2 had started: the service dropped map 2 to retrack map 1 (which failed), then picked map 2 up again a minute later. An earlier map now never replaces a later tracked one.
- **Team order swapped mid-series.** getLive listed C9 first on map 1 and FURIA first from map 2. Settled map winners (`results.json`), FORGE (`forge.json`), the series quotes and the score are all for team 0, so a swap could flip them. Each live event's teams are now put in the order first seen for the match (`service.pin_order`, saved in `data/live/<match>/teams.json`); a different team pair is left alone. Tests: order and score kept after a swap, input not mutated, other teams untouched. Now `service.feed_sides` reads the map's sides from one feed window (`gameMetadata`) before tracking it, waits for the next discovery on a 204, and refuses a feed whose teams aren't getLive's; a resumed `Game` keeps its saved selection's sides. Checked on Riot's real feed: KC v MVK map 3 with getLive's stale sides comes back corrected; the unplayed FURIA map 5 gives None.

Checks: `uv run pytest -q tests/live` (new: Kalshi alias match, feed sides swap/wait/refuse, resume keeps sides), full suite and ruff below. CI cost: none (a few unit tests); the worker makes one extra feed request per new map. Not verified on a live production match; the worker picks this up at its next refresh (`deploy/live/refresh.sh`, every 6 hours or on boot). Earlier maps lost today are not recoverable from the published files.

## 2026-10-16 — Fee-aware Kelly sizing and strategy bankroll

Choose the feasible route with best log growth rather than cheapest price regardless of depth. ## 2026-10-16 — Show preparing matches on Live

- The running worker reads Riot's schedule once per discovery (one extra request per minute), publishes known fixtures from 75 minutes before their start through four hours after it, and keeps itself awake for them. ## 2026-10-15 — Current-model page backtest and immutable alert identity

Branch `prediction-history-fix`, base main `3837c5b` (PR #57). User requested the Predictions backtest always use the newest model while saved calls and alerts stay fixed.

- `prediction_backtest.rebuild` creates separate current-model copies of reconstructed calls, using only ratings before each UTC match day and a separate before-day cutoff 12 hours out. The page, rebuilt-call scorecard, market/edge tables and reconstructed results use these copies. The original prediction log and genuine saved forecasts are never changed by replay; `predictions.json` remains the saved log and `backtest.json` publishes the separate replay. Unrateable reconstructed fixtures are excluded rather than retaining an old-model probability. Recalculation occurs on every build, including fetch-disabled/fallback builds, so no stale model cache survives a release.
- Pin new alerts to the canonical backed team. Carry the first alert's probability/price/edge/time through team-order changes; orient carried market/12h prices to the refreshed order. Align results to a frozen call's team order; a late reschedule cannot unfreeze it, and a blank feed cannot erase a known result. A provider-ID/round-label change reuses a unique exact team-pair/start/league fixture, preserving the original identity and first alert; ambiguous fixtures are not merged. Settlement resolves the pinned team, with a legacy side fallback; an absent team is not misgraded.
- The live alert record attributes the Oct 14 KT–BNK alert to KT, but the original pre-match GitHub notification #45 backed BNK (3–1 winner). A one-time main-only recovery step reads versioned S3 logs chronologically from the first notification hour, restores exact original terms for that fixture, pins BNK and saves a recovery audit including the previous corrupt record/source version. It never infers terms from the result or rounded issue text, and fails before publishing if original evidence is unavailable/inconsistent. Subsequent restores are a no-op. Saved probabilities are untouched. The local AWS SSO token is expired; recovery uses the already authorized deploy role in Publish, not a local credential change.
- Checks so far: 68 focused tests passed in 1.14 s, including model-change replay, separate 12h cutoff, source immutability, team-order/result reversal, provider-ID continuity, identity-based settlement and evidence-based/idempotent recovery. ## 2026-10-14 — Single-offset full replay: experiment specification and checks

Continues `international-market-diagnostic` on main `36a1e6e`; research only, production Elo source unchanged.

- Full replay replaces existing regional updates (`league_share=0`) with absolute learned offsets; fixed-scale and fitted-scale arms isolate probability calibration. Same rookies, active-player priors, transfers, duration and player updates; fits at completed-event day-end become available next day. Baseline reproduces the DB and saved price calls. Forecast-only controls reproduce the previous scaled correction and show a fixed-scale correction also helps (−0.0128 versus −0.0175 with scale).
- Initial replacement: 2022+ day-before maps fixed +0.0017 versus baseline, scaled +0.0046 (both intervals span zero). Native 2022+ scaled replay is significantly worse within other leagues (Elo +0.001523, FORGE +0.001147); fixed is within uncertainty across guarded slices. Neither demonstrates beating Kalshi. The original gain does not survive this straightforward replacement.
- **Follow-up specified before its run:** one offset table with ordinary per-game updates plus completed-event absolute refits, ridge 4 shrunk toward its current values; fixed probability scale. This preserves fast adaptation without a separate forecast correction. No new parameter grid; all outcomes are already viewed development evidence.
- **Follow-up results:** 2022+ day-before 1,006 maps: 0.585987 versus 0.591756, −0.0058 (series-cluster −0.0108 to −0.0007; event-cluster −0.0100 to −0.0007). Major-v-major −0.0026 spans zero; entry −0.0024 also within noise. Native 2022+ cross-league −0.003755 (−0.005280 to −0.002201), but within-other-league Elo +0.000387 (+0.000059 to +0.000710) is significantly worse and therefore fails the safeguard. Other native slices within game-bootstrap noise. No adoption.
- **Market:** primary 76 series 12h out: recalibrated 0.5442, Elo 0.5510, Kalshi 0.5349; Δ vs Elo −0.0068 (−0.0229 to +0.0090), vs market +0.0094 (−0.0846 to +0.0946). All 95 at close: Δ vs Elo −0.0184 (−0.0346 to −0.0020), vs market +0.0264 (−0.0540 to +0.1010). No demonstrated Kalshi edge; close is secondary, four viewed events and no Worlds. Results in `learned_offsets_report.md`, downstream slices in `learned_offsets_guards.md`.
- **Attribution/limitations:** correction-only fixed-scale gain −0.0128 (−0.0232 to −0.0023), so much of the previous −0.0175 survives without scale changes. Folding into one effective offset is exact for a frozen rating snapshot; feedback replay and switching fit inputs from day-start to native pre-game are different interventions, and their effects are not separately identified. Event blocks/entry, unknown homes, baseline hindsight and series-format limitations remain. The recalibration follow-up was developed after viewing replacement results; it is exploratory. No Bayesian/team-effect model, production DB rebuild, live deploy or full all-season/five-family adoption suite was performed.
- **Regression localization:** recent home-league change slice (first 10 starter appearances after a known move, promotions/relabels included): 19,751 games, mean Δ +0.000700 and sum +13.826; rookie without recent move: 7,976, +0.000292 / +2.327; neither: 9,521, −0.000182 / −1.737. These disjoint descriptive slices account for the overall excess loss but do not establish causality or a fix. No model selection or retuning on these labels.
- **Runtime/checks:** five-arm research run 36.36 s; baseline wrapper 3.95 s versus fixed replacement 4.63 s and recalibration 4.64 s (~0.69 s extra including fits); native check runner 34.46 s before roster attribution, 37.33 s with it. No new production/CI work or dependencies. Nine replay tests 0.81 s including startup, plus one roster-slice bookkeeping test (0.95 s including startup); exact DB baseline and all 95 series/43 map-1 calls at both cutoffs reproduce within 1e-9/1e-12. Final full check: `uv run pytest -q` 418 passed in 6.62 s; `uv run ruff format --check`, `uv run ruff check`, `git diff --check` passed. All 21 new/changed relative links resolve; replay code hash matches its audit and every fit predates availability. Production package and DB scripts are unchanged; no site build needed for research-only code.

## 2026-10-13 — Kalshi alert: a rebuild no longer re-alerts the same match

Branch `alert-repeat`, based on `main` (3da95b3).

- **Bug.** `schedule.update_log` rebuilt every match that hadn't started from the day's prediction, carrying over Kalshi's prices but not the `alert` / `paper_alert` records (`alerts.record`). So each publish run's alert step saw an already-alerted match as new: it commented "New: 1 match" on the day's issue again and re-recorded the alert at the current price, breaking "alerted once, at its first price". Seen on issues #28 (Shopify Rebellion v FlyQuest, three repeats on 7 Oct) and #45 / #48 (BNK FEARX v KT Rolster, a repeat after each of the three merges to main on 12 Oct). The hourly prices job never calls `update_log`, so it didn't repeat.
- **Fix.** `update_log` keeps both records (`schedule.ALERT_KEYS`) on a match it refreshes. Test `test_update_log_keeps_alerts_so_a_rebuild_doesnt_alert_again` fails without the fix.
- **Left:** alerts already re-recorded keep their later price (BNK FEARX v KT Rolster: first alerted at 35¢, +9.5, on 12 Oct; the log now holds 36¢, +8.2, from 13 Oct 02:24). The paper record scores them at that price; not corrected by hand.
- **Benchmark check: floating-point noise no longer fails it.** This PR's check failed on domestic Elo (live) "worse" by +0.0000 (+0.0000 to +0.0000): base and head dumps, run on different machines, differ by about 1e-16 per game (up to 2e-12 for Form and FORGE), and over 16,810 games every resample landed above 0. `compare_benchmarks._verdict` now needs the interval to clear `TOLERANCE` (1e-6) on one side; test `test_floating_point_noise_is_within_noise`. PR #47's "better" by −0.0000 on international team Elo was the same noise.
- Checks: `uv run pytest -q`; `ruff format`, `ruff check`; the per-row differences measured on this PR's downloaded dumps.

- **First look at real lines (12 Oct, 176 matched on 8 matches):** we lean to the under on most kills lines; the apps' lines track longer averages than our 4-game half-life (Yike: last 12 maps 2.9 kills, lines 11–12.5 over three maps, ours 8.6). Not evidence either way until lines settle.
- Checks: `uv run pytest -q`; `ruff format --check`, `ruff check`; workflow YAML parsed; live reads of all three apps; `PREDICTIONS_FETCH=0` site build with the live prediction log (local DB games through 1 Oct, so local projections miss the last 11 days); screenshots at 1440, 1280 and 390px (a 390px iframe, since headless Chrome won't go below about 500px); impeccable detector (one advisory, fixed). Not run: the CI jobs themselves.

## 2026-10-12 — Player props: projections and line collection (Phase 0, research)

Branch `props`, based on `main` (a2f7d37). Nothing on the site or in a published metric changes.

- **Data:** `match_players` gains `champion`, `kills`, `deaths`, `assists`, `cs` (Oracle's Elixir full-game totals) for every game; `player_stats` lacks all of LPL 2025 for want of snapshots, and those games are where the lines are.
- **Model (`prometheus/props.py`):** per stat a Poisson regression (scaled logs of player, team, opponent and league-role pace rates, role, the result and result × role) for the mean in a win and a loss, a negative binomial dispersion per stat, role and result, mixed by the Elo win chance; Pick6 fantasy points as a normal per result; series totals summed over every result sequence of the best-of. States are taken before the series' first map.
- **Backtest (`scripts/evaluate_props.py` → `docs/props_report.md`, about 3 min):** half-life 4 and prior 3 chosen on 2022–2023 with models fit through 2021 (the grid is flat: sums 11.634–11.646); refit through 2023; 63,330 player-maps of 2024+ LCK, LPL, LEC, LCS and international games, bootstrap over 2,636 matches. One-map log score vs the player's last 10: kills −0.054 (−0.057 to −0.050), assists −0.082, deaths −0.059, CS −0.025; vs the player's rate alone kills −0.025, assists −0.057, deaths −0.036, CS +0.001 (−0.001 to +0.003, no gain); vs the same model without the result term kills −0.015, assists −0.047, deaths −0.022, CS −0.003. Fantasy CRPS vs last 10 −0.56. ECE at a stand-in line: kills 0.007, assists 0.018, deaths 0.024, CS 0.036 (projects low). Series (vs summed last-10): maps 1–2 of a Bo3 kills −0.109, maps 1–3 of a Bo3 as played kills −0.127, of a Bo5 −0.139, ECE for kills 0.005–0.018.
- **Lines (`prometheus/prop_lines.py`, `scripts/collect_props.py`):** no free historical prop lines exist (searched: PrizePicks and Pick6 scrapers give current lines only; OddsPapi and The Odds API carry no LoL player props; Kalshi lists no LoL player markets). Direct reads work: Underdog `v1/over_under_lines` (the `beta` paths answer 426) with per-side multipliers and implied probability; Pick6's page decoded from its turbo stream (default view only); PrizePicks' partner API (the main API is behind a bot check; partner API returned 429 after about 5 calls, then 166 lines). A live read on 2026-10-12 got 90 Underdog (kills, assists, fantasy on maps 1–3), 16 Pick6 (kills) and 166 PrizePicks lines (kills, standard/demon/goblin, including combo props).
- Plan, edge bar and next steps in `current_state.md` Future work.
- Checks: `setup_db.sh` rebuilt; `evaluate_props.py --out docs/props_report.md`; one live `collect_props.py` read of each source; `uv run pytest -q`; `ruff format --check`, `ruff check`. Not run: the other benchmark scripts (no published metric changed; `match_players` only gains columns).

## 2026-10-12 — Fixed duration curve: integrated with paired benchmark guards

Branch `elo-duration-curve`, based on research commit 99f3d6a (production baseline a2f7d37). Changed Elo’s duration evidence to the frozen `0.825 + 0.175 * tanh((1800 - seconds) / 300)` curve. K, rookies and regional updates are unchanged. Record calls the original `power_winner_score` explicitly, preserving published GLORY. Explicit legacy steepness parameters still select the original power curve for research. Form/FORGE weights and all per-league slopes were refitted; metric descriptions and reports were refreshed. The touched README also had obsolete GLORY placeholder features and a logistic-regression claim; checked `types.py`, the SQL view and `regression.py` and corrected them to the actual twelve features and standardized linear model.

- [Full integration comparison](../research/duration_curve_adoption.md): international Elo/FORGE −0.004513 (0.628278 → 0.623765), cross-league −0.001591 (0.601504 → 0.599913), other-league Elo −0.001742 and FORGE −0.001044 (0.610065 → 0.609021), all significant. Domestic changes are inside noise; no protected forecast, season, AURA or market measure is significantly worse, with zero unpaired units. GLORY, Record, Luck and guarded AURA values are unchanged. Sunset GLORY+ loses some other-half fit (unguarded).
- [Completion gates](../research/duration_curve_gates.md): the forecast gains survive team-pair/day clustering and earlier-only fitting. On 1,444 map-1 markets with both weights frozen through 2024, all-map loss delta +0.000039 [−0.001540, +0.001648] is inconclusive.
- The ordinary `evaluate_markets.py --offline` failed on the incomplete local candle cache (no priced rows). Added `rebuild_cached_markets.py` to reconstruct every prediction on the same saved priced fixture cohort: 2,398 series and 1,444 maps, never reusing old odds. The standard market comparison shows no significant regression. Prices/outcomes are unchanged; no network fetch was attempted.
- Checks: private DB rebuilt from read-only raw inputs; `evaluate_metrics.py --write-weights` and `--check-weights` (no warnings), `evaluate_season_stats.py`, AURA base/head dumps, complete `compare_benchmarks.py` (pass), full `uv run pytest -q` (329 passed), repository Ruff format/check, offline site build with saved log/prices, rendered-page spot checks and final Markdown/diff checks. No templates, CSS or JS changed.
- Limits: curve discovered through a retrospective ablation; raw annual imputation and initial-year warmup remain inherited. Cluster intervals use team-pair/day as a series proxy. Market confirmation is on a saved priced cohort, without fetching new candles. No merge or live deployment; renamed-team continuity remains separate research.

## 2026-10-12 — Conditional tempo and fixed duration curve experiments (research)

Branch `elo-tempo-surprise`, based on `main` at a2f7d37. Added opt-in research runners for online duration expectations, a bounded symmetric update curve, attribution controls, chronological calibration and offline market reconstruction. K, rookie treatment, regional links, published computation and weights remain unchanged. Reports: [conditional experiment](../research/elo_tempo_surprise.md), [attribution and validation](../research/elo_tempo_validation.md), [fixed curve follow-up](../research/elo_tempo_fixed.md).

- Conditional tempo improves 2022+ other-league FORGE by −0.000823 [−0.001177, −0.000483], but loses to an identical curve with a fixed 30-minute center by +0.000722 [+0.000532, +0.000913]. The evidence supports curve shape rather than conditional tempo.
- The frozen fixed curve is `0.825 + 0.175 * tanh((1800 - seconds) / 300)`. Earlier-only 2022+ evaluation improves other-league Elo −0.002372 [−0.002844, −0.001940], FORGE −0.001702 [−0.002111, −0.001315], international −0.005098 [−0.008978, −0.000848], and cross-league −0.001431 [−0.002637, −0.000183]; domestic changes are inconclusive. All-season guard comparisons have no significant regressions.
- On 2,398 cached 2025+ market series, with both methods' weights frozen through 2024, fixed versus baseline is −0.001033 [−0.002711, +0.000659]; no market slice is significantly worse. Current-code reconstruction agrees with direct routing within 1.87e−14; cached old predictions are unused.
- Checks: private DB rebuilt from read-only raw inputs (205,701 match rows), baseline replay exactly reproduced 205,688 rated team-games; seven chronology, symmetry, control, bounds and routing tests; full suite independently rerun after handoff: 327 passed in 5.39s; repository Ruff format/check, 19 local Markdown links and `git diff --check` passed.
- Limits: retrospective ablation-origin candidate, not untouched prospective confirmation; inherited annual-file imputation and early state initialization limits remain. Fixed-curve intervals use game resampling; conditional clustering does not establish fixed-curve clustering. Cached market series were checked, but map-1 markets, production weight refresh and a candidate site build were not. No defaults adopted or deployment performed.

## 2026-10-12 — Blue side in Elo and our calls: tested, not shipped

Branch `blue-side`, based on `main` (a2f7d37).

- **Elo (`prometheus/elo.py`):** `compute_elo_records` options `side_advantage` (points blue's expected score counts above red's in the update; a number or by tier, major leagues and international events vs the rest) and `side_rate` (learn each tier's advantage online from blue's changes), off by default; stored ratings stay neutral. `load_elo_games` now also returns `side`, which the default replay ignores. `scripts/research/elo_variants.py` passes it through.
- **Research:** `scripts/research/blue_side.py`, report `docs/research/blue_side.md`, tests `tests/research/test_blue_side.py` and three in `tests/test_elo.py`. Grids fixed in advance, tuned on 2014–2021 (domestic + within-other-league Elo log loss), held out 2022–2026 against published Elo (rookie discount on). One advantage (20): Elo domestic −0.0001 (−0.0004 to +0.0002), international +0.0002 (−0.0010 to +0.0014), other leagues +0.0001 (−0.0001 to +0.0003), cross-league +0.0003 (−0.0002 to +0.0008); by tier (30/20) and learned (rate 0.05) the same within ±0.0005, the learned one significantly worse cross-league (+0.0004, +0.0001 to +0.0008). FORGE moves the same way. Fails the forecast rule.
- **Calls:** game 1's side isn't knowable before a match: Leaguepedia's `team1` was blue in game 1 in 56% of 394 logged matches matched to Oracle's Elixir (LAS 90%, VCS 87%, the rest near half). Later games follow the loser's pick, whose preferred side changes by season (loser on blue in major-league games: 92% in 2025, 21% in 2026), and a side term moves a series chance by under 0.001. No map-1 market test was possible without game 1's side. Nothing changed in calls.
- Checks: `uv run pytest -q`; `ruff format`, `ruff check`; the replay with defaults is unchanged (`test_side_advantage_off_by_default_and_without_side_column`).

## 2026-10-12 — Form's stat weights: ridge, fewer stats, other-league and pooled fits (not shipped)

Branch `form-weights`, based on `main` (a2f7d37).

- **Options** (`prometheus/form.py`, defaults unchanged): `fit_form_weights(..., C=)` (L2 penalty on standardised gaps), `scores(..., other_weights=, majors=)` (rows whose home league isn't a major scored with a second set). Unit tests in `tests/test_form.py`.
- **Research** (`scripts/research/form_weights_fit.py`, report `docs/research/form_weights_fit.md`): grid fixed in advance (C 1e6/1/0.1/0.01 × all stats / no first-objective flags / gold + per-10 rates × fit on major, other-league or pooled games, plus blends of other-league and major weights at 0.25/0.5/0.75). Tuned on ≤2021 (Form weights and FORGE curves fit only on those seasons), majors chosen by domestic FORGE, then other leagues by FORGE within other leagues. Held out 2022–2026 (weights fit on every other season, as the backtest does), paired bootstrap vs the current fit.
- **Result.** Every design moves log loss by 0.0003 or less. Ridge 0.0000. Chosen (majors pooled, other leagues pooled without first flags): domestic FORGE +0.0001 (−0.0002 to +0.0004), international 0, domestic Form alone +0.0003 (−0.0004 to +0.0010), within other leagues −0.0003 (−0.0004 to −0.0001); pooled everywhere the same. It passes the rule's letter within other leagues; not shipped for a gain of about 2% of the rookie discount's (left for the combined decision). The negative weights survive ridge.
- Checks: the research run (about 2.5 min), `uv run pytest -q`, `ruff format`, `ruff check`.

## 2026-10-12 — Elo: experience past the rookie window (tested, not shipped as default)

Branch `elo-experience`, based on `main` (a2f7d37).

- **Options** (`prometheus/elo.py`, `compute_elo_records`, defaults unchanged): `rookie_curve="log"` (the discount falls with log(1 + games): D × (1 − log(1 + g) / log(1 + N))); a veteran discount `veteran_penalty` growing from `veteran_games` to twice that, or from `veteran_years` years after a player's first game over three more years. Like the rookie discount, both count only when team ratings are formed, never in a player's own rating. Tests in `tests/test_elo.py`.
- **Research** (`scripts/research/elo_experience.py`, report `docs/research/elo_experience.md`): grids fixed in advance, tuned on 2014–2021 (domestic + within-other-league Elo log loss), held out 2022–2026 against published Elo (linear 200/50, moves ×2 for 25 games). Best log discount D 250, N 200 (train 0.62275 vs 0.62382). Held out: Elo cross-league −0.0023 (−0.0038 to −0.0008), Elo within other leagues −0.0005 (−0.0011 to +0.0001), FORGE domestic +0.0000, FORGE within other leagues +0.0000, domestic and international Elo +0.0001 (none significant). The veteran discounts barely move the train objective (−0.00002 to −0.00004 on top of the log discount): by games (500, 100) cross-league −0.0019 (−0.0034 to −0.0003), domestic Elo +0.0004 (−0.0001 to +0.0010); by years (6, 100) cross-league −0.0026 (−0.0043 to −0.0008), within other leagues Elo −0.0009 (−0.0016 to −0.0002), the rest not significant.
- **Verdict.** No FORGE gain on any section. The log discount passes the forecast rule on cross-league Elo calls only (nothing significantly worse); not yet checked paired on the market benchmark's cross-league series, which a default change should be. Defaults unchanged here, so it can be combined with the other assumption branches.
- Checks: `uv run pytest -q`, `uv run ruff format`, `uv run ruff check`; the research run (about 25 minutes).

Branch `league-move-direction`, based on `main` (a2f7d37).

## 2026-10-12 — Elo K by tier and time away: tested; time away passes, K doesn't

Branch `elo-k`, based on `main` (a2f7d37). No published metric changed (defaults unchanged, so it can be combined with the other assumption branches).

- **Why.** A team's Elo change over its last 10 games predicts its next result beyond Elo (z +8.9 within other leagues, +2.7 majors, in sample), so ratings looked too slow.
- **Options (`prometheus/elo.py`, off by default):** `league_factors` (a game's league → multiplier of its players' moves; league offsets still move by `league_share` of the unscaled change) and `away_factor` / `away_days` (a player back after time away moves `1 + away_factor × min(days away / away_days, 1)` times as much, Glicko-style). Tests in `tests/test_elo.py`.
- **Research (`scripts/research/elo_k_tier.py`, report `docs/research/elo_k_tier.md`).** Baseline is the published Elo (K 20, rookie discount on). Grids fixed in advance, tuned on 2014–2021 (domestic + within-other-league Elo log loss), held out 2022–2026. Uniform K 28: Elo other leagues −0.0014 (−0.0018 to −0.0010), cross-league −0.0034, but FORGE domestic +0.0007 (+0.0002 to +0.0012). K by tier (chose 28 / 28): the same gains, FORGE domestic +0.0007 (+0.0002 to +0.0013) and international +0.0040 (+0.0009 to +0.0070), so it fails. Faster Elo duplicates what Form already adds inside a league. Time away (a = 2.0, the grid's edge): Elo other leagues −0.0006 (−0.0009 to −0.0003), FORGE other leagues −0.0003 (−0.0005 to −0.0000), cross-league −0.0009 (−0.0017 to −0.0001), domestic −0.0001 and international +0.0002 (both not significant). It passes the forecast rule on its target sections, but the gain is small. K by tier + time away fails like K by tier.
- **Checks:** `uv run pytest -q`, `uv run ruff format`, `uv run ruff check`. Not run: `evaluate_metrics.py` and the market benchmark with time away as the default (left for the combined change), and larger `away_factor` values (outside the fixed grid).

## 2026-10-12 — Elo and FORGE assumptions: residual checks and a test list

Folded into branch `forge-combined` (the residual checks that led to the 2026-10-12 tests). Docs only.

- **Checks** (scratch scripts, not committed): replayed published Elo in memory with the current code (rookie discount on; the local DB predates it), then logistic fits of the result on the blue-minus-red Elo gap plus one feature at a time, by tier (major domestic 18,657 games, within other leagues 69,056, cross-league 2,130; 2016 on, curves fit on the same games, so in sample and a screen only; about 30 features × tier tests, so treat z near 2 as weak).
- **Findings.** Recent Elo change (last 10 games) predicts results beyond Elo: z +8.9 other leagues, +2.7 majors, still there without players under 50 games (worth 0.40 and 0.23 of a point of gap per point of change). Mean career games z +3.1 other, +3.4 cross; 400+ game veterans z −2.5 in other leagues. Players moved into a major in their last 30 games z −2.7; moved down z +3.9. Days since a starter's last game z ≈ −2. Exact-five familiarity z +2.0 other, +1.6 majors. Fitted Elo slopes 0.0085 majors / 0.0103 other per point (400-point curve: 0.0058), stable by year; blue side +0.16 log-odds in majors. Nothing by game number in a series beyond what ρ covers; nothing early in a season.
- **Outcomes** of the six tests that followed are in the entries above and in `current_state.md` Future work.

- Checks: `uv run pytest -q`; `ruff format`, `ruff check`; new tests (same dumps give zero differences, the table matches `edge_record` on the same calls, an old-format base borrows prices, neither skips with a note, the verdict ignores betting rows). Local run: base dump from the saved `frames.pkl` (`--reuse`) against a fresh `--offline` run on this worktree's DB, identical calls, all differences 0, exit 0; and against a copy with calls shrunk 20% toward 50%, value-bet return −7.7 to −10.9 points with intervals excluding 0.
- Limit: not run in CI yet (workflow unchanged; the next PR's comment shows it). The difference of returns compares different bet sets when the calls differ, so it answers "which version bets better", not a per-bet pairing.

## 2026-10-12 — Market benchmark: alerts by lead time; alerts lose their 36-hour cap

Branch `alert-leads`, based on `main` (a2f7d37). Question: is the alerts' 36-hour cap (`alerts.MAX_HOURS`) costing us earlier, better bets?

- **`scripts/evaluate_markets.py`:** new "Alerts by lead time" section (`alert_leads_section`, `call_at`, `at_lead`; `ALERT_LEADS` 12/24/36/48/72 hours and `ALERT_EDGE` 0.05, fixed before the run). At each lead our call is rebuilt from games before the bet's day (the rest of the report uses games before the match day, which for a 48-hour bet would know games played after it), against the market's price and the ask then: Δ log loss, our weight beside the market, value bets above 5 points at the 7% taker fee (ROI, CLV), on each lead's own matches and on the matches priced at every lead to 48 hours. Tests for `call_at` and `at_lead`.
- **Report rerun** (`docs/market_report.md`, fetching 141 series and 223 map-1 price histories the local cache lacked; the market lists were not refreshed, so matches run to 30 Sep). The rest of the report moved because of the rookie discount and aliases since 6 Oct (2,527 series with a closing quote, from 2,486).
- **Results, FORGE within a major league, same 460 matches, 12 → 24 → 36 → 48 hours:** Δ log loss +0.0025 → +0.0000 → −0.0012 → −0.0023 (all within noise); our weight beside the market 0.43 → 0.51 → 0.53 → 0.56; value bets 175–192 a lead, ROI after fees +13.8% / +11.8% / +13.2% / +12.4% (every interval spans 0); CLV +1.47 → +2.65 → +2.48 → +3.30 points (all above 0), 59% → 67% beating the close. At 72 hours (416 own matches, near when markets open) ROI +22.6% (+2.6% to +43.6%), CLV +3.43, but it is one cell of many. FORGE within other leagues: CLV rises the same way (+1.01 → +2.41) but ROI stays about −1%. So earlier bets are no worse and their CLV is higher; nothing here says the cap should be 36 hours.
- **Alerts: no upper lead limit** (user's call after these results). `alerts.MAX_HOURS` is gone; `select` takes FORGE calls 6 or more hours out, so a match qualifies as soon as Kalshi quotes it (prices with a spread over 10¢ are never stored, so no alert fires on them). Issue text, workflow comment, `test_alerts.py` (a 72-hour match now qualifies), tech.md, deployment.md and current_state.md updated. Kalshi has a usable quote (spread ≤ 10¢) on 83% of timed series markets 12 hours out, 57% at 36, 50% at 48 and 37% at 72 (median spread 2¢ → 5¢ → 6¢; half the markets open within 77 hours of the start).
- **The 6-hour minimum, checked** (one-off run with `ALERT_LEADS` set to 1/3/6/12/24, offline cache, not committed). FORGE within a major league, same 501 matches: our weight beside the market 0.55 at 24 hours → 0.47 (12) → 0.46 (6) → 0.40 (3) → 0.38 (1), Δ log loss −0.0018 → +0.0037 (all within noise); value-bet ROI +12.3% / +13.7% / +24.0% (+1.2% to +45.3%) / +16.4% / +13.8%, the others spanning 0. Every slice: CLV turns negative inside 6 hours (a bet near the close pays the ask against a mid, so CLV there is biased down). Within other leagues ROI falls to about −9% inside 6 hours. So the market does sharpen over the last hours and the minimum is a reasonable cutoff, but the data can't tell 6 from 3 for major-league FORGE. That the late gain is lineups is untested (Leaguepedia has no starters before a match).
- **Limits:** calls are rebuilt with weights fit on data including these matches; the backtest's call knows every game before the bet's day, while live data (Oracle's Elixir) can lag a few days; the same matches appear at every lead, so the rows are not independent tests.
- Checks: `uv run pytest -q` (all pass, after the alert change too); `ruff format` and `ruff check`; the report run twice (offline cache, then fetching); the major-league FORGE rows matched between the two.

## 2026-10-08 — Elo: rookie discount for new players; forecast rule judges the games a change targets

Branch `new-player-elo`, based on `series-odds` (6467028).

- **Rule (AGENTS.md, tech.md, product.md):** a forecast change now ships when the paired-bootstrap interval excludes 0 on the games it targets (domestic, international, cross-league or within other leagues) and none of those sections gets significantly worse. Before, only a domestic gain counted, which no change aimed at non-major leagues could show.
- **Elo (`prometheus/elo.py`):** a new player counts `ROOKIE_PENALTY` (200) lower in their first game, the discount falling linearly to nothing after `ROOKIE_GAMES` (50) games, applied when team ratings are formed and never stored in the player's own rating; a player's first `PROVISIONAL_GAMES` (25) games move their rating `PROVISIONAL_FACTOR` (2) times the team's change. Options on `compute_elo_records` (also `new_player_offset`, tested and rejected). `evaluate_metrics.py`'s team-Elo reference (no rosters) runs without them. Weights and curves refit (`--write-weights`): other-league Form weight 0.513 → 0.363 (Form had been standing in for the rookie effect), the rest within 4%.
- **Why:** teams with players under 10 career games lose more than Elo expects (about 5 points of win chance per extra such player). `scripts/research/elo_new_players.py` (report `docs/research/elo_new_players.md`) tuned on 2014–2021 and held out on 2022–2026 against Elo without the discount: within other leagues Elo −0.0139 (−0.0157 to −0.0123), FORGE −0.0123 (−0.0136 to −0.0110), cross-league Elo −0.0208 (−0.0254 to −0.0161); domestic Elo −0.0006 (−0.0013 to +0.0002), FORGE −0.0003, international −0.0010 (not significant). Starting new players lower instead is worse everywhere (league means drift down with turnover).
- **Full backtest, before → after (`compare_benchmarks.py` on local dumps, same CSVs):** within other leagues Elo per league 0.6271 → 0.6129 (−0.0142, −0.0156 to −0.0129), FORGE 0.6222 → 0.6102 (−0.0120); cross-league 0.6194 → 0.6019 (−0.0175, −0.0209 to −0.0139); domestic Elo 0.6321 → 0.6315, FORGE 0.6294 → 0.6290, international 0.6280 → 0.6283 (all within noise). Season stats and AURA unchanged except the sunset GLORY+ (r with the other half's win % −0.005, significantly worse; unguarded). Markets (offline cache, 2,398 series): series log loss −0.0057 (−0.0095 to −0.0019), FORGE major −0.0003, map 1 −0.0032 (−0.0064 to −0.0000).
- **Kalshi betting** ($1 a series 12 hours out, 7% taker fee, 1,752 priced series, offline cache, before → after): value bets at a 5-point edge +$36.50 → +$46.96 (ROI +3.9% → +5.4%, −4.0% to +15.3%); in other leagues −$2.75 → +$16.25 (−0.4% → +2.6%); at a 10-point edge +$7.75 → +$37.38; back our pick −$6.90 → −$3.13. Every ROI interval still includes 0.
- Checks: DB rebuilt (`setup_db.sh`); `evaluate_metrics.py --write-weights --out docs/metric_backtest.md`, `--check-weights` (no moves); `evaluate_season_stats.py --out docs/season_stats_report.md`; `evaluate_aura.py` and `evaluate_markets.py --offline` to temp dumps (reports not committed: AURA unchanged; the market report's offline coverage differs from the committed online one); research report rerun with the old Elo pinned (identical held-out numbers); `PREDICTIONS_FETCH=0 build_site.py`; `uv run pytest -q`, `ruff format --check`, `ruff check`.

## 2026-10-08 — Pool depth in FORGE: tested, not shipped

No metric, report or page changes. Branch `pool-depth`, rebased onto `main` after #43.

- **`scripts/research/evaluate_pool_depth.py`** (new; report `docs/research/pool_depth_report.md`) with **`scripts/research/champion_pools.py`** (rolling pre-game pools per player-game, exact against a brute-force count; tests `tests/research/test_champion_pools.py`). Fixed before scoring: 180-day window, depth = gap in the starters' mean effective number of champions, missing pool = gap 0; controls experience (log games) and depth + experience. On `evaluate_metrics.py`'s games with out-of-year curves.
- **Results (variant minus published, log loss):** domestic + depth −0.0001 (−0.0004 to +0.0002), 2025 on −0.0001; international + depth +0.0007 (−0.0004 to +0.0018), depth + experience +0.0030 (significantly worse); within other leagues + depth −0.0022 (−0.0027 to −0.0017), + experience −0.0032 (−0.0038 to −0.0026), depth over experience −0.0000. Fails the forecast rule in the majors; outside them the signal is experience, concentrated where a team's starters average under 10 games in the window (−0.0121). It led to the Elo rookie discount (entry above). These numbers are against Elo before that discount; not rerun after it.
- Checks: `uv run pytest -q`, `ruff format --check`, `ruff check`; the report regenerated after the last script change.

## 2026-10-08 — Series odds by beta-binomial; series independence and fearless draft (research)

Branch `series-odds`, based on `main` (ba81227).

- **Series odds change.** `schedule.series_probability(p, best_of, rho)` is now a beta-binomial (each series draws its chance from a Beta with mean p and within-series correlation rho; rho = 0 is the old independent-games formula), with `SERIES_RHO` by tier: 0.054 when both teams' home leagues are major, 0.127 otherwise (`series_rho`). Used by the Predictions page (new and refreshed calls; frozen calls keep their saved chance), the game logs (`gamelog.add_calls` now also returns `home` / `opp_home`) and `evaluate_markets.py`. The Predictions page's series-chance note says so.
- **Why it ships (forecast rule):** fit on 2018–2024, scored on 2025–2026 clean-league series: −0.0037 series log loss [−0.0058, −0.0017]; major-league series −0.0021 [−0.0045, +0.0002] (not worse); Bo5 −0.0097. On Kalshi's priced series −0.0028 [−0.0061, +0.0002]. One-game calls are unchanged.
- **`scripts/research/series_independence.py`** (new; report `docs/research/series_independence.md`, tests `tests/research/test_series_independence.py`): series grouped as the game logs group them, game 1's call, tier from the two teams' home leagues, a fearless label per league-split read off champion repeats, leagues with ≥1% tied series left out (missing games). Fits rho by year, tier, best-of and format; compares rho models out of sample (one rho, by tier, by league shrunk to tier, per team from games the starters played together, tier + new-roster flag) on series log loss and every game's result; tests game-number slopes, the lead effect, champion burn and pool depth under fearless and not.
- **Findings:** series calls were overconfident (Bo5 slope 0.68) though game-1 calls are calibrated. Per-league and per-team rho add nothing over tier out of sample. Fearless changes neither rho (0.118 vs 0.135) nor the lead effect. Pool depth adds about +0.11 log-odds per sd in both formats (after experience); next as a FORGE input.
- **Stale market cache:** the first Kalshi comparison showed our other-league series calls underconfident (slope 1.45). The main checkout's `data/markets/frames.pkl` dates from 2026-10-06 15:28, before the per-league Elo curves and other-league FORGE; `evaluate_markets.py --offline` rebuilt it in the worktree, and the slope is 0.82. The main checkout's cache is still the old one. `docs/market_report.md` is not regenerated here: an offline run covers different matches than the committed report (prices for newly rated matches aren't cached), so the PR's benchmark check is the like-for-like comparison.
- Checks: new tests (`predict`'s series chance checked to within the rounding of `p_game`; published formula against a closed-form Bo3 and the research's beta-binomial, `series_rho` tiers, `predict` uses the major rho; research: rho = 0 against the old formula, a 200,000-series simulation, per-series rho, rho recovered from simulated series, fearless labels); `PREDICTIONS_FETCH=0 build_site.py` builds and the Predictions note renders; `uv run pytest -q`, `ruff format --check`, `ruff check`.
- Limit: fearless and the 2025–26 calendar coincide (2024's 203 fearless series are the only overlap). The roster-games bands were first looked at on all years before the out-of-sample test (the new-roster cutoff comes from 2018–2024 only).

## 2026-10-08 — Starting lineups for predictions: checked, not built

No code, metric or page changes.

- **Source.** Leaguepedia's Cargo tables have no starters before a match: `MatchSchedule` has no lineup fields, `TournamentPlayers` is the event roster (substitutes included, no starters), and `ScoreboardPlayers` / `MatchScheduleGame` are filled in after each game, after our call freezes at the match start.
- **Value, best case.** Calls use each team's main roster after its previous game (`main_elo`); the backtest uses the game's real starters (`pre_match_elo`). On major-league games from 2022 (8,967), Elo with the real starters vs the main roster, same fitted slope: the two differ by more than 0.5 points in 11.0% of games (16% in 2022, 8% in 2025–26), by a median of 25 Elo points (p90 74). On those games, log loss is 0.6072 with the main roster and 0.5964 with the starters: a gain of 0.0108, with a bootstrap 95% interval of [−0.0009, 0.0234]. That is 0.0012 over all games. The best case is not significant, and part of it is substitutions made between games of a series, which a series call can't use. Not worth building without a pre-match source.
- Checks: the Cargo field lists queried with the bot login; the measurement was a throwaway script on a fresh `setup_db.sh` DB, not committed. `uv run pytest -q`.

## 2026-10-08 — Cleanup: site builder split into prometheus/site/, shared evaluation helpers, cache headers, wider lint, shorter steering docs

No metric, report or page changes (checked byte for byte, below).

- **`scripts/build_site.py` split** (1,890 → about 330 lines; `main()` only) into `prometheus/site/`: `config` (metric registries, column configs, paths, limits), `render` (Jinja environment, filters, small pages), `registers` (register rows, home and rankings pages, FORGE register), `teams`, `players`, `predictions` (page, log update, Kalshi alert) and `gamelogs`. Each top-level block moved verbatim (an `ast` script); the paths and limits tests patch (`OUTPUT_DIR`, `PREDICTIONS_LOG`, `KALSHI_ALERT`, `MARKET_PRICES`, `GAME_LOG_SERIES`) are read as `site_config.X` at call time. Tests import the modules instead of loading the script by path.
- **Shared evaluation helpers** in `prometheus/evaluation.py`: `resamples` (bootstrap row positions over rows or clusters, one draw per resample from one seeded generator, so callers get the draws their loops made), `interval` and `ece`. Used by `paired_bootstrap`, `paired_correlation_bootstrap`, `compare_benchmarks._rows_bootstrap`, `evaluate_aura.roster_bootstrap` and the market benchmark's ROI and blend intervals; the three copies of ECE are gone (`evaluate_aura.ece`, `evaluate_markets.calibration`, `research/evaluate_gol_full.ece`, which now wraps the shared one).
- **Cache headers.** `deploy_site.py` uploads CSS and JS with `public, max-age=31536000, immutable` (every link carries `?v=`; checked no template or stylesheet loads one without it) and fonts with 30 days, one `aws s3 cp` per header; a full upload sends those files again with their header. Files already in the bucket get the header when they next change, or on a `full_upload` dispatch.
- **JS:** `esc`, `ROLES` and the league mark (`leagueMark`) moved into `names.js` from `rankings.js`, `search.js` and `entry.js` (entry keeps its own tooltip mark), with Node tests.
- **CLI:** `prometheus weights` prints the published FORGE blend and Form weights (it only printed "Fetching weights..."); `rankings --league` defaults through `None` (B006).
- **Lint:** ruff now selects `E9, F, I, B, UP, C4, PERF, RUF` (ignoring `RUF001-003`, `B905`, `RUF005`, `PERF401`, `UP042`); 41 safe fixes (mostly `datetime.UTC`), the rest by hand: `raise ... from`, `| None` hints, unused loop variables, loop values bound into closures that run inside their loop. Optional `.pre-commit-config.yaml` (ruff 0.16.10, as locked).
