## 2026-10-07 — Maintenance: safe prediction log, data freshness, per-file downloads, two data fixes, faster DB build, CI trims

From a repo review (correctness, CI speed, cost, cleanliness); the review's remaining items are in current_state's Future work ("Maintenance left from the 2026-10-07 review").

- **Prediction log can't be wiped by an S3 error.** `publish.yml` read the log with `aws s3 cp … || echo "No saved prediction log yet."`, so any S3 error started a new 30-day log that the save then wrote over the real one. New `scripts/prediction_log.sh restore|save`: restore fails on any error but a 404 and records the ETag; save refuses a log with fewer matches than the backup; the hourly prices job saves with `--if-unchanged` (skips when a publish wrote since its restore). The backup bucket is versioned (checked). Checked: restore against the real backup (12,473 matches), a missing key (starts a new log) and a 403 (fails); every save case against a fake `aws` (grow, shrink refused, concurrent write with and without `--if-unchanged`).
- **Data freshness is visible.** The home page's title line and the Predictions page say "Games through <date>" (newest game in the DB); `build_site.warn_missing_results` prints a `::warning::` when the prediction log has settled matches more than 36 hours old that the data lacks (`schedule.missing_results`; on today's local data: games through 1 Oct, 113 settled matches to 5 Oct missing); `alerts.select` skips a call whose ratings lack a team's settled result (the market has seen it). Live calls for 4–5 Oct had been made with data through 1 Oct.
- **Per-file downloads.** `gdown --folder` fails the whole folder when Drive refuses any one of its 13 files. New `scripts/download_data.py` lists the folder and downloads each file on its own: the current year's (and last year's in January) when `data/raw` has the rest, every file with `--full` (`REFRESH_DATA=full`, Mondays in CI). A file replaces the old one only if it parses and has newer games, so a refused, broken or not-yet-updated file changes nothing and doesn't count as the day's download. On 7 Oct the single 2026 file downloaded (games through 7 Oct) while folder downloads were refused.
- **Two data fixes, shipped with `benchmark-override` as accuracy fixes (owner's call).** (a) Rows Oracle's Elixir leaves without a `teamid` (500 team rows in 2026; 413 team names across years, 19 of them with an id elsewhere in the file) get that id or `name:<teamname>` (`002_add_matches.fill_team_ids`): games with one team row 1,850 → 13. (b) `matches.year` is the calendar year of `date`, not Oracle's Elixir's season label (autumn promotion and qualifier games filed under the next year, e.g. 152 team rows of Aug–Sep 2026 labelled 2027; the Form page showed 44 "2027" rows, now 0). Paired benchmark against `main` on the same CSVs and Kalshi cache (`compare_benchmarks.py`), each alone: (a) FORGE within other leagues −0.0005 log loss (−0.0006 to −0.0004), Elo per league within other leagues −0.0006 (−0.0008 to −0.0004), but GLORY r with the other half's win % −0.002 (−0.003 to −0.001, guarded: fails), domestic Elo +0.0002 (+0.0000 to +0.0003) and market series called by Elo outside FORGE +0.0064 (+0.0025 to +0.0104), both unguarded; (b) market calls on FORGE major-league series −0.0028 (−0.0057 to −0.0004) and map 1 −0.0011 (−0.0020 to −0.0003), but FORGE within other leagues +0.0003 (+0.0001 to +0.0005) and AURA new-team r −0.011 (−0.020 to −0.003), both guarded: fail. Together: domestic and international FORGE and Elo (live) within noise; AURA new-team r −0.012 (−0.020 to −0.004). Reports regenerated (`metric_backtest.md`, `season_stats_report.md`, `aura_report.md`) and weights refit (`--write-weights`; FORGE blend weights moved under 3%). `market_report.md` left as is: an `--offline` run scores fewer markets (2,398 series with a close against 2,486) than the committed online one. The e2e minimum-games test now picks its threshold from the data (Cloud9 Challenger's promotion games moved to 2016).
- **Faster DB build, same content.** `002_add_matches.py` reads only the columns it uses and writes with `utils.insert_rows` (one `executemany`), as does `bootstrap_elo`; `load_rosters` builds its dict in one pass. Locally 63 s → 28 s. Checked against `main`'s DB table by table (row count, content and SQLite storage type): identical; only the row order of the four ingest tables changed (files now read in sorted order; before, the filesystem's order).

Checked in a fresh `.venv`.

The prices job's busy check counts only `main` publish runs (`--branch main`). The publish build runs the full-DB e2e tests and `--check-weights` alongside `build_site.py` (about 30–50 s less wall time; one step, fails if any does), skips the site artifact on PRs, and is skipped on PRs that touch only `docs/` and root Markdown (`changes` job). ## 2026-10-07 — Ratings before a day from one read (market benchmark 615 s → 138 s), PR benchmark check, CSVs downloaded once a day

- **Speedup, no value change.** `elo.LatestElos(method)(date)` gives exactly `get_latest_elos(method, date)` from one read of the Elo and offset tables (22 ms a call instead of about 1 s). `schedule.RatingsBefore(states)(day)` gives exactly `ratings_before(states, day)`, cached by day, using it; the market benchmark (`our_calls`), the prediction log's rebuilt calls (`build_predictions`) and `scripts/oneoff/extend_log.py` use it. `evaluate_markets.py` on the same DB and Kalshi cache: 615 s on `main`'s code → 138 s; both runs' `frames.pkl` tables identical (`assert_frame_equal(check_exact=True)`: 2,486 series rows, 1,561 map rows, counts and unmatched names) and the two reports byte-identical. New `tests/e2e/test_ratings_before_e2e.py` checks exact equality on 13 cutoffs spread over the data (fixture DB, and the full DB in CI; an earlier version took every fifth game day and ran 26 minutes on the full DB).
- **PR benchmark check.** `--dump DIR` on `evaluate_metrics.py`, `evaluate_season_stats.py` and `evaluate_aura.py` writes the per-game losses, per team-season halves, AURA's held-out data and calibration predictions (reports unchanged: byte-identical with and without it). New `scripts/compare_benchmarks.py BASE HEAD` pairs the dumps and exits 1 when a guarded value is significantly worse (published forecasts' domestic, international, cross-league and within-other-league log loss; GLORY split-half and other-half r; AURA split-half, new-team and roster r; AURA ECE over 0.01). New `.github/workflows/benchmarks.yml` (PRs only): `prepare`, then `bench` as a parallel matrix (head and base on their own runners and DBs, the same cached CSV entry, the three scripts at once; base dumps cached by base commit and CSVs), then `compare`, which posts one sticky comment (`scripts/benchmark_comment.sh`) and fails unless the PR has the `benchmark-override` label; label changes reuse the stored verdict. No baseline until this merges. The market benchmark is in it too: `evaluate_markets.py --offline --dump` (our call and result per series and map 1; offline reads only the cached Kalshi data, so both sides score the same markets; offline run identical to an online one, tables and report) as its own matrix group (head/base × core/markets, so it overlaps the others), guarded on our series log loss (all and FORGE major) and map-1 log loss; a faked +0.04 FORGE major-league shift fails both series rows, untouched slices stay within noise. New `kalshi-cache` job in `publish.yml` (main only, off the deploy path): `evaluate_markets.py --update-cache` fetches newly settled markets (series and map 1, about 130 missing now) and saves an Actions cache entry keyed on the cache's contents; its first run fills an empty cache (about 4,900 requests, ~45 min) and saves progress even if it times out. Until that cache exists, the market comparison is noted as missing.
- **CSV downloads.** Every daily run from 2 to 7 Oct (and every push and PR) had all three Google Drive attempts refused ("Too many users have viewed or downloaded this file recently"; reproduced locally), about 96 s of a 184 s DB step. Now a `main` build downloads only when the cache has no `oracles-elixir-day-<data day>` entry (data day from 10:00 UTC, the cron, after Oracle's Elixir updates) and saves one after a successful download; PRs and later runs build from the cache. `setup_db.sh` waits 10 s between attempts instead of 30 and 60. `check` now runs alongside `build` (deploy waits for both).
- Checks: market benchmark before/after as above; same-code dumps compare with exit 0; faked FORGE, GLORY, AURA r and ECE regressions exit 1, pure noise exits 0 (`tests/test_compare_benchmarks.py`); the parallel benchmark step run locally (43 s for all three, exit 0); `uv run pytest -q`; `ruff format --check`, `ruff check`; actionlint on all workflows. Not verified until GitHub runs them: the matrix and artifacts, the base-dump cache, the sticky comment and label reuse (first real comparison only once this is on `main`), the once-a-day download (first `main` run after merge should download or retry, the next should skip Drive).

## 2026-10-07 — Changed-only deploys, faster player reads, ruff and a pre-data CI check, research archived

- **Deploy.** `scripts/deploy_site.py` replaces the `releases/<run id>` upload and the copy to `current/`: it hashes `output/`, compares with `s3://$DEPLOYMENT_BUCKET/deploy/manifest.json`, uploads only changed and new files straight into `current/`, deletes removed ones, always re-sends `kalshi.json` and `predictions.json` with `max-age=300`, then writes the manifest. The manifest is deleted before any file changes, so a failed run makes the next one upload everything; `full_upload` on a manual dispatch forces that. The footer's "Last updated" date is gone (the home page's title line keeps it), so unchanged pages hash the same. Rollback: re-run an earlier run's deploy job (artifacts last 7 days) or restore S3 versions and delete the manifest (`deployment.md`). Game-log JSON is not gzipped at upload: CloudFront already compresses it, and unchanged files are no longer re-sent.
- **Build speed.** `prometheus/player_tables.py` (`PlayerTables`) reads `match_players`, `player_stats`, `matches` and the player Elo table once and joins in pandas; `load_aura_games`, `get_player_history` and `load_player_games` take it (`tables=`) and return exactly what their SQL joins did (`DataFrame.equals`, row order included). The three reads: 26.6 s → 13.4 s together. `team_rosters` built from one pass over sorted rows (5.1 s → 0.6 s) and player stints aggregated in pandas with an indexed player lookup (`player_pages_and_rows` 4.6 s → 2.1 s), outputs equal. Whole build, alternating runs against `main` on a loaded laptop: 69–88 s → 51–53 s (user CPU 61–77 s → 45–47 s). Parallel rendering of team and player pages in forked processes was tried and dropped: the render phase took 7–12 s serially and 12–28 s with 4 workers (forking a process holding several GB; sys time +16–30 s). Not done: `read_sql` through plain `sqlite3` (Future work).
- **Tooling.** ruff (format, and check with E9, F and I; `pyproject.toml`) replaces black and pylint. `pytest` config: `testpaths`, an `e2e` marker on the two e2e modules. CI `check` job (ruff format --check, ruff check, the whole suite) runs before the data download and gates `build`; `build` reruns `-m e2e` on the full DB instead of the whole suite. `test_gol_coverage`'s offline test no longer needs a built DB (empty in-memory `matches`). No pre-commit config.
- **Cleanup.** Removed `regression._fit_glory_model`, `_evaluate_model` and `matches.get_team_averages_frame` (their tests now cover `fit_glory_pipeline` and `team_season_averages`); matplotlib moved to a `research` dependency group. `elo._offsets_table` and `_players_table` are used, so they stay. 17 research scripts moved to `scripts/research/`, `extend_log.py` and `backfill_market_prices.py` to `scripts/oneoff/` (imports and test paths updated), 16 research reports to `docs/research/`. Work-log entries before 2026-10-06 moved to `work_log_archive.md` (every entry was under a quarter old, so this rotates by size: 164 KB → 75 KB).
- **Formatting.** `ruff format` and `ruff check --fix` (import order) over the repo in a separate commit, listed in `.git-blame-ignore-revs`.
- Checks: `uv run pytest -q`, and with `db/` and `data/` moved aside; loader outputs compared with the SQL versions; `PREDICTIONS_FETCH=0 KALSHI_PRICES=0 build_site.py` then `diff -r` of `output/` against the base branch's build (only the footer line on every page and `kalshi.json`'s timestamp differ); `deploy_site.py --dry-run` on a built site; actionlint on `publish.yml`. Converting a mover's rating by standing in the old league: worse everywhere (domestic Elo +0.0072, within other leagues +0.0107, cross-league +0.0256), matching the earlier test. Team-only link offsets: non-international cross-league games (EMEA Masters, cups, promotion) move a second offset at share 0.25 that counts in ratings, while players changing league convert by the international offsets alone.
- **Backtest** (paired, share fixed in advance): cross-league −0.0123 (−0.0151 to −0.0095), from 2022 −0.0187 (−0.0232 to −0.0141); domestic Elo −0.0002 (−0.0005 to +0.0000), FORGE −0.0002 (−0.0003 to −0.0000); international +0.0012 (−0.0004 to +0.0028); within other leagues +0.0002 (−0.0001 to +0.0004). `evaluate_metrics.py` cross-league 0.6193 → 0.6070; FORGE within other leagues unchanged (0.6217).

With `main`'s weights the Elo change alone gives +0.0017 and the weight refit −0.0001. The loss sits in LEC Versus, the KeSPA Cup and the LCK; likely cause (not verified): a strong ERL's link offset holds part of its strength, so its promoted players arrive slightly underrated.

## 2026-10-06 — Kalshi fees: no maker fee on LoL markets

- **Finding.** Kalshi's `/series` data lists `fee_type: quadratic`, `fee_multiplier: 1` for `KXLOLGAME` and `KXLOLMAP` (also CS2 and Valorant), while NFL, NBA and NHL game series list `quadratic_with_maker_fees` (MLB at 0.5). Kalshi's API docs: `quadratic` is the General Trading Fees Table only; `quadratic_with_maker_fees` adds maker fees (0.25 × taker; 0.5 for `quadratic_with_combo_maker_fees`). So on LoL, takers pay ceil(0.07 × C × P × (1 − P)) and resting orders pay nothing. The market report's "Maker 1.75%" rows were wrong; its "None" rows were the maker numbers all along. The 7% taker rate used by the alerts and Table IV is right.
- **Change.** `markets.fee_rates` / `fetch_fee_rates` read the rates from Kalshi's series data; `evaluate_markets.py`'s betting tables use them (`fee_rows`: taker, a maker row only when the series has maker fees, and None, labelled as also covering resting orders), with a fallback to taker 7% / no maker fee if Kalshi can't be reached. The report text says where the rates come from. `docs/market_report.md` rewritten with `--reuse` (only the betting section changed).
- Checks: `uv run pytest -q`; new `test_fee_rates_follow_the_series_fee_type`, `test_fee_is_the_general_trading_fee_rounded_up_to_the_cent`.

## 2026-10-06 — Fitted Elo curves within non-major leagues (one per league); worktree setup

- **Change.** Predictions (`schedule.game_probability`) and game-log calls (`gamelog.add_calls`) between two teams of one non-major league used the textbook Elo curve (400 points per 10× odds, 0.00576 log-odds per point). They now use that league's fitted slope (`league_curves.json`, 93 leagues, via `forge.other_league_weight`), or the pooled `other_league_elo_weight` (0.01241, `forge_weights.json`) for a league without one. Both are refit by `evaluate_metrics.py --write-weights`. Major-league and cross-league calls are unchanged.
- **Per-league slopes** (`evaluation.shrunk_league_slopes`): each league's logistic slope, shrunk toward the pooled slope by empirical Bayes (DerSimonian-Laird), so no constant is tuned. Leagues whose results the gap separates perfectly get no slope of their own. Examples: LCK CL 0.0076, LFL 0.0087, PRM 0.0087, CBLOL 0.0100, VCS 0.0103, LDL 0.0109, LJL 0.0132, PCS 0.0134, LAS 0.0136.
- **Benchmark.** New "Within other leagues" section in `evaluate_metrics.py` (`evaluation.other_league_games`, with `season_homes` factored out of `cross_league_games`; `out_of_year_league_probabilities`; unit-tested), 67,928 games, curves fit out of year: textbook 0.6453, one curve 0.6285, per league 0.6268. One curve vs textbook −0.0168 (−0.0182 to −0.0154); per league vs one curve −0.0017 (−0.0021 to −0.0012); from 2022 on −0.0168 (−0.0187 to −0.0149) and −0.0020 (−0.0028 to −0.0013). Domestic and international forecasts are untouched. Judged on the games it affects, since no major-league benchmark covers them. ## 2026-10-06 — Home league = most-played league; forecasts use the main roster; accuracy-fix rule

- **Rule** (user, 2026-10-06): a change that corrects which data a forecast uses (home league, the roster that stands for a team, a mislabelled event) without changing how the metric is computed is an accuracy fix. It ships when it is significantly better on the games it affects and domestic and international log loss don't get significantly worse. Added to `AGENTS.md` Conventions and `tech.md`.
- **Elo** (`compute_elo_records`): home league = the non-international league the team has played most this calendar year, last year's home counting as one game, a tie keeping the current home (was: the league of its last non-international game, so the KeSPA Cup, Demacia Cup, EMEA Masters or WSCI relabelled teams). New `main_elo` per team-game (`001_create_tables.sql`): the rating of the team's main roster (starters of its last home-league or international game), so an academy lineup under the team's name at a cup no longer stands in for it. `get_latest_elos` and `get_season_elos` use `main_elo` and the stored `home_league`. Tests: `test_a_cup_keeps_the_home_league_and_main_roster`, `test_home_league_moves_to_the_most_played_league`, `test_latest_elo_rates_the_main_roster_after_a_cup`.
- **Backtest, same games, new vs old DB** (paired): cross-league, every league −0.0011 (−0.0021 to −0.0001; major v other −0.0038, −0.0070 to −0.0005; other v other −0.0006; major v major +0.0005, −0.0003 to +0.0014). Elo (live) domestic +0.0001 (−0.0002 to +0.0003), international +0.0003 (−0.0002 to +0.0009); FORGE domestic +0.0001 (−0.0001 to +0.0002), international +0.0003. Passes the accuracy-fix rule. The backtest scores each game's real lineup, so it can't see the main-roster fix.
- **Market benchmark, our old vs new calls on the same 2,180 series:** −0.0075 (−0.0149 to −0.0011); calls that changed (933) −0.0175 (−0.0338 to −0.0020); series formerly called cross-league (171) −0.057 (−0.130 to −0.006); FORGE series −0.0003 (−0.0021 to +0.0010). Biggest gains: KeSPA Cup (39 series, −7.0 nats), LCK (−2.7), Prime League (−2.4), LPL (−2.0), LCK Cup (−1.5), EMEA Masters (−1.3). 159 series moved from "Elo, same other league" to cross-league (mostly EMEA Masters, now teams keep their ERL) and 54 to FORGE. Against the market at 12 hours: Elo across leagues +0.159 → +0.085 (215 series), Elo within other leagues +0.049 → +0.039, FORGE +0.003 (−0.014 to +0.019).
- Teams currently labelled with a cup or cross-league event: 93 → 11 (teams that only play those). Median rating change for teams active since June 2026: 5 Elo points.
- Checks: `setup_db.sh` rebuild; `evaluate_metrics.py --write-weights --out docs/metric_backtest.md` (elo_weight 0.004864 → 0.004828, form_weight 0.5012 → 0.5050, cross-region 0.008726 → 0.008738); `evaluate_markets.py --out docs/market_report.md`; `PREDICTIONS_FETCH=0 build_site.py` (2,087 team, 4,854 player pages); `uv run pytest -q` (204 passed). Didn't look at the built pages in a browser (no template or CSS change).

## 2026-10-06 — Cross-league benchmark for every league; minor-league offset tests (no metric change)

- **Benchmark.** `evaluation.cross_league_games` (unit-tested) and a new "Cross-league, every league" section in `evaluate_metrics.py`: Elo (live) on every game between teams whose home leagues differ (home = most-played non-international league that season), at any event. `docs/metric_backtest.md` rerun; earlier sections unchanged. Current Elo: 9,432 games, log loss 0.6205 (major v major 0.6364, major v other 0.5984, other v other 0.6239; international events 0.6173, EMEA Masters/cups/promotion 0.6214).
- **Why the market beats us across leagues.** Its 171 cross-league series (`data/markets/frames.pkl`) lose most at the KeSPA Cup (8 series, 9.6 nats of the gap), EMEA Masters (39, 6.5), and LCK/LPL league games labelled cross-league (12, 4.9). Causes: (1) `compute_elo_records` sets a team's home to the league of its last non-international game, so a cup (KeSPA Cup, Demacia Cup, EMEA Masters) makes it a "KeSPA Cup" team until its next league game, and predictions use the cross-league curve; (2) cup games never move offsets, so EMEA Masters doesn't link the ERLs and the KeSPA Cup doesn't link LCK with LCK CL; (3) in 2026 LCK teams fielded academy rosters under their main names at the KeSPA Cup, and predictions use a team's last roster, so Gen.G was rated 1,403 before a match its main roster played (we said DN SOOPers 98.9%, the market 11.5%).
- **Tests** (scratch harness replaying `compute_elo_records`, not committed; paired bootstrap vs published Elo; cross-league set chosen by calendar-year homes, 9,606 games). Home = most-played league this year (last year's home seeded with one game), links only at internationals: domestic +0.0001 (−0.0002 to +0.0003), international +0.0003 (−0.0002 to +0.0009), cross-league −0.0012 (−0.0021 to −0.0002). Plus any cross-home game moves offsets at share 0.5: cross-league −0.0173 (−0.0204 to −0.0142; other v other −0.0226, major v other −0.0079) but domestic +0.0008 (+0.0001 to +0.0015). Share 0.25 for non-international links (0.5 kept at internationals): cross-league −0.0137 (−0.0159 to −0.0113), domestic +0.0004 (−0.0001 to +0.0009), international +0.0012 (−0.0004 to +0.0029), within other leagues −0.0001; but from 2022 on domestic +0.0009 (+0.0001 to +0.0016) and international +0.0025 (+0.0002 to +0.0050). Share 0.1: cross-league −0.0082, international from 2022 +0.0016 (+0.0001 to +0.0030). Keeping the majors' offsets for international events only: international +0.0034 (+0.0008 to +0.0059). Linking only games between two non-major leagues (share 0.25): domestic +0.0004 (+0.0001 to +0.0008), international +0.0020 (+0.0006 to +0.0033). Prior weight 5 for last year's home: no change.
- Reading: linking minor leagues makes their offsets right against each other, and players who move up then carry those offsets into a major (a rating converts across leagues by offset), which hurts major-league forecasts. Nothing passes the significance rule; Elo is unchanged.
- Checks: `uv run pytest -q` (201 passed); `evaluate_metrics.py --out docs/metric_backtest.md`.

## 2026-10-06 — League-offset tests for cross-region forecasts (scratch, no metric change)

- Scratch harness (not committed) replays `compute_elo_records` with rosters (reproduces the DB's pre-game Elo exactly, 9 s per replay) and scores the backtest's games with the published out-of-year Elo curve. Settings chosen on international cross-region games before 2022 (443), confirmed on 2022 on (536); published cross-region log loss 0.6295 (0.6465 early, 0.6155 late).
- **Elo knobs** (league share L × team's own international move m; L=0.5, m=1 is published): best early was m 0.5, L 1.0 (early −0.0063, late +0.0003, all 0.6268). m 1, L 0.75: early −0.0019, late −0.0006. Larger L hurts both periods and domestic games (L 2: domestic +0.0016, late +0.0045). Gains before 2022 don't carry over; nothing ships.
- **Bradley-Terry league correction** on top of published Elo (per-league log-odds term refit monthly on earlier cross-region games, L2 penalty λ, recency half-life h). Early-chosen λ 30, h 365–730: late −0.0010 (−0.0032 to +0.0013) / −0.0019 (−0.0049 to +0.0012); all −0.0007 / −0.0012, intervals include 0. Post-hoc longer memory (h 1460) −0.0016 to −0.0018 over all, also inside the noise. The fitted corrections now are about ±6 Elo points (LCK +7, LPL +5, LEC −6, LCS −6): the offsets are close to calibrated on average.
- **League part scaled** (gap = Elo + (k−1) × league-mean gap, curve refit on international games): k 1.5 −0.0000 vs published; it only recovers the +0.0017 an international-only curve costs. The 1.5× league coefficient found earlier doesn't forecast better out of year.
- Conclusion: nothing passes the significance rule; FORGE stays Elo-only across leagues and Elo keeps L 0.5. The market's cross-league edge (+0.16 log loss, 171 series) is mostly minor-league cross-league series (about 33 are major vs major), which `evaluate_metrics.py` doesn't score. Player-transfer links weren't tested.

## 2026-10-06 — Form in cross-region forecasts (scratch test, no metric change)

- Question: can Form inform cross-region odds, where FORGE uses Elo alone? Scratch harness on `evaluate_metrics.py`'s frame (979 international cross-region games), Form weights fit out of year, curves fit out of year, paired bootstrap against the published cross-region Elo curve (0.6295).
- Range restriction: the league-relative Form gap has sd 0.46 in cross-region games vs 0.75 domestically (both sides are usually top seeds), and correlates 0.68 with the Elo gap. Opponent-adjusted raw Form (not league-relative) correlates 0.92 with Elo there, so it mostly restates Elo.
- Variants (log loss, difference vs published): domestic FORGE weights applied across regions 0.6409 (+0.0114, +0.0050 to +0.0180, significantly worse); Elo + league-relative Form fit on international games 0.6314 (+0.0019, −0.0027 to +0.0066); Elo + raw Form 0.6319 (+0.0024, +0.0010 to +0.0038); Elo + Form scaled by its league-season spread 0.6307 (+0.0012, −0.0039 to +0.0063); Elo refit on international games only 0.6312.
- Fitted on all international games, Form's coefficient given Elo is negative: −0.40 (SE 0.20), nearly all from 2020 on (−0.54, SE 0.23). Splitting the Elo gap into a league part (gap between the two leagues' mean Elo this season) and a within-league part: league 0.0109 (SE 0.0013) vs within 0.0073 (SE 0.0012) log-odds per point, and Form's coefficient drops to −0.26 (SE 0.24). Reading: Form was standing in for league strength the league offsets under-count; it carries no extra team signal across regions. The split itself didn't beat Elo out of year (+0.0015, −0.0039 to +0.0071).
- Nothing shipped; FORGE stays Elo-only across leagues. Follow-up in current_state Future work.

## 2026-10-06 — Player Elo margins of victory: 28 variants, none ships

- **Question** (user): player Elo scores a win by game length; would gold difference, kill share, gold share, towers and so on do better? Tested by four subagents on a shared harness, under the significance rule.
- **Harness** (`scripts/elo_variants.py`, tests in `tests/test_elo_variants.py`): replays player-built Elo in memory with any margin function (winner's score in (0.5, 1], vectorised over the games with both teams' `match_stats`), reproduces the DB's `pre_match_elo` exactly when the DB's league classification is used (the local DB predates WSCI's move to `INTERNATIONAL_LEAGUES`, 503 team-games from 13 Sep differ; variants are compared with a current-code replay), and scores Elo (live) and FORGE exactly as `evaluate_metrics.py` does (max per-game difference 0), refitting Form and the FORGE blend out of year for each variant. Every variant is tuned on seasons 2014–2021 only and reported on 2022–2026 (7,720 domestic and 536 international games) against the published Elo on the same games, paired-bootstrap 95% intervals. Baseline held out: Elo live 0.6232 domestic, 0.6158 international; FORGE 0.6208 domestic.
- **Results** (held-out domestic Elo-live Δ; below 0 is better). Gold (`docs/elo_variants_gold.md`, 8 candidates, 120 points): best on training was gold share (K 30), −0.0026 in sample but −0.0004 (−0.0020 to +0.0012) held out, FORGE +0.0006 (−0.0004 to +0.0016); gold per minute −0.0007 (−0.0025 to +0.0011). Kills (`docs/elo_variants_kills.md`, 5 + control, 66 points): all worse, kill difference significantly (+0.0014), every one significantly worse for FORGE. Objectives (`docs/elo_variants_objectives.md`, 8 candidates, 140 points): towers, dragons, barons, a composite and first objectives; none better held out, baron difference significantly worse everywhere, every one significantly worse for FORGE (dragons and barons pile up in long games). Combined (`docs/elo_variants_combined.md`, 7 candidates, 131 points): a dominance index learned on 2014–2021 from gold, kills, towers and shortness (gold and shortness carry it) with K 28: −0.0009 (−0.0026 to +0.0009), international −0.0052 (−0.0126 to +0.0026), FORGE +0.0002. Also: no margin at all (binary result) is significantly worse than game length (+0.0009 domestic, +0.0056 international), so a margin helps, but nothing beat game length; K 28 alone is not a gain (+0.0004; FORGE significantly worse). About 460 parameter sets in all, so a few uncorrected "significant" intervals are expected by chance; the three international gains in the combined family (re-tuned game length −0.0025, −0.0044 to −0.0005) are of that kind and decide nothing on their own.
- **Verdict:** keep the game-length margin with K = 20. No published metric changed. Checks: `uv run pytest -q` (all pass, including the harness's 25 tests).

## 2026-10-06 — Demacia Cup and WSCI count as international events

- **Bug.** The first alert run on main posted nothing: every Demacia Cup match was an Elo call. Oracle's Elixir files the Demacia Cup Global Invitational as league `DCGI` and the World Star Challengers Invitational as `WSCI`; neither was in `INTERNATIONAL_LEAGUES`, so a team's last games there made it the team's home league (12 team slots on the live log under DCGI, 2 under WSCI). Effects: same-league FORGE calls became Elo calls (no alerts), those teams left the FORGE page, and the Elo league offsets treated the events as leagues. WSCI had done this since mid-September.
- **Fix.** `DCGI` and `WSCI` added to `INTERNATIONAL_LEAGUES`. They now link regions like Worlds and MSI, and teams keep their regional home league.
- **Benchmark** (DB rebuilt on Oracle's Elixir data through 2026-10-05, before and after, `evaluate_metrics.py` pipeline, per-game paired bootstrap on the 17,752 games in both). Domestic (16,773): FORGE 0.6291 → 0.6291, Player Elo 0.6319 → 0.6319, Team Elo 0.6343 → 0.6343, all Δ 0.00000. International (979): FORGE 0.6295 → 0.6295 (Δ −0.00001, −0.00002 to +0.00001), Elo likewise. The change only touches games from mid-September; 14 DCGI/WSCI games join the international test set. Shipped as a data-classification fix with no measurable effect on the forecasts (user: ship unless the forecasts drop).
- Refit the forecast weights (`--write-weights`; largest move 2.5%, from five more days of data) and refreshed `docs/metric_backtest.md`.
- Checks: `get_latest_elos` home leagues back to LPL/LCS/LCK/LEC for the Demacia Cup teams, none left under DCGI or WSCI; a full local build from the live S3 log with live fetches made the Demacia Cup's same-league matches FORGE calls and wrote one alert (Shopify Rebellion v FlyQuest, FORGE 43% against a 36¢ ask, +7.4), not posted; `uv run pytest -q` (136 passed, e2e included). Google Drive refused the CSV download, so the 2026 CSV came from the CI backup in S3.

## 2026-10-06 — Lead-time ladder, betting backtest and daily Kalshi alerts

- **Backtest additions** (`scripts/evaluate_markets.py`): a lead-time ladder (market at the close and 6/12/18/24 hours before, against our day-before call, on 485 major-league and international series with a quote at every lead) and a betting section ($1 at the ask 12 hours out, Kalshi's fee `ceil(rate × C × P × (1 − P))` at 7% taker, 1.75% maker or none; strategies fixed before the run). `--reuse` rewrites the report from saved tables; progress lines with times.
- **Results.** FORGE (474 series): log loss level with the market from 6 to 24 hours out (Δ −0.001 to −0.004), and FORGE's weight beside the market's price is 0.53–0.59 with intervals above 0 (e.g. 12 hours: 0.53, 0.02 to 1.16); at the close it is 0.27 (−0.12 to 0.72). Betting on FORGE calls: back our pick −3.5% after taker fees; value bets above 0 / 3 / 5 / 10 points +4.5% / +5.1% / +12.9% / +20.3%, every interval spanning 0 (above 5 points: −5.0% to +31.2%, 215 bets). Other leagues (Elo): value bets −20.1% (−30.1% to −10.3%). Not significant for FORGE, and several strategies were tried.
- **Alerts.** New `prometheus/alerts.py` and `build_site.write_kalshi_alert`: FORGE calls 6–36 hours out whose series chance beats the ask by more than 5 points, using only prices read in that build; each alert recorded once on its log entry; `data/kalshi_alert.json` holds the GitHub issue (matches table with Kalshi, Predictions and team links; running paper record after fees; how it is chosen; @-mention). `markets.attach_prices` now stores each side's ask (`buy_costs`); `markets.fee` shared with the backtest. Workflow: `issues: write` and a *Post the Kalshi alert* step (main only, edits the day's issue on a rerun, closes the previous day's, never blocks the deploy). Decided with the user: 5-point bar, GitHub issue as the email, off-season invitationals count.
- **Stall.** One backtest run sat at 100% CPU for 41 minutes before its first line; with `VECLIB_MAXIMUM_THREADS=1` the same work took 8 minutes (cause not confirmed beyond the sample showing BLAS threads).

## 2026-10-06 — Kalshi's prices on the Predictions page

- **Data.** New `prometheus/markets.py` (Kalshi parsing, quotes and aliases, now shared with `scripts/evaluate_markets.py`) and `schedule.attach_market_prices`: each build reads Kalshi's open `KXLOLGAME` markets once and stores the market's series chance (`market`: p, spread, at, ticker) on every logged match that hasn't started, matched by our team names and the nearest start within 18 hours; thin quotes (spread over 0.10) are skipped; `update_log` keeps an upcoming match's last price, and a started match keeps its last one. Any Kalshi error or `KALSHI_PRICES=0` leaves prices as they were. Added `INTZ e-Sports` to the aliases.
- **Dynamic?** Not possible on the static site: Kalshi's API answers a request carrying another site's Origin with 403, so the browser can't read it. Prices are as fresh as the daily build.
- **Site.** Fixture register: a Kalshi column (pair, ink-2, beside One game, phone-hidden, linking to the match's market page on Kalshi; URL format taken from Kalshi's indexed match pages, as kalshi.com refuses scripted requests) in upcoming registers and an ink-2 caret on the duel bar at the market's chance (both registers; results via the bar's tooltip). Note 5 explains the price and the benchmark result. Designed with the impeccable skill inside the Almanac fixture register.
- **Checked live:** 32 open markets (16 matches) on 2026-10-06; every name but two resolved; the one upcoming match also in the local log (Cupid Esports v Fuego) took Cupid 83.5% from a 0.83/0.84 book, oriented correctly.
- Docs: DESIGN.md (fixture register, Predictions page), tech.md (pipeline, Predictions, tests), deployment.md (`KALSHI_PRICES`), current_state, README.
- Verification: `uv run pytest -q` (130 passed on this branch, e2e included; new `tests/test_markets.py`, `update_log` and `attach_market_prices` tests, `fixture_row`/register market test). A build from a scratch copy of the log with made-up prices on 25 rows (screenshots only; the real log was not touched), Playwright at 1440 and 390px: no page errors, no horizontal scroll, caret readable at 99–1 and 4–96.
- Limits: Kalshi's Developer Agreement on republishing prices not read (the page refused the fetch); prices up to a day old; no comparison row in the scorecard yet.

No verified live source, prior/draft model, or trading edge. No exact series IDs; calendar-year splits and league/day clusters are approximations for series grouping.

## 2026-10-04 — Market benchmark: our calls against Kalshi's prices

- **Source.** Kalshi's public API (no key) lists settled LoL markets back to Dec 2025: `KXLOLGAME` (series winner, one contract per team; 2,768 settled series) and `KXLOLMAP` (single maps), from the majors to ERLs and academies, plus price history (hourly candlesticks) on live and historical endpoints. Polymarket also lists per-match markets, many with no order book; not used yet.
- **`scripts/evaluate_markets.py`** (new): fetch and cache markets and candlesticks (`data/markets/`), parse each market's rules text (teams, event, Eastern start; day-only starts as midnight), match names (`TeamMatcher` + `MARKET_ALIASES`, 14 pre-rebrand names), rebuild our call from `ratings_before(day)`, take the best-of and check the result from our games, price the market at the close and 12 hours before (bid-ask mid, spread ≤ 0.10), and report accuracy, Brier, log loss with paired bootstraps by slice, a logistic fit on both log-odds, calibration, and map 1 against our one-game chance. Report: `docs/market_report.md`.
- **Results** (2,156 series with a closing quote). Major leagues and international, 12 hours out: FORGE 0.5816 vs market 0.5822 log loss (Δ −0.0006, −0.016 to +0.015, n 708), a tie; at the close +0.013 (−0.004 to +0.029). The market gains 0.020 log loss over its last 12 hours. Other leagues (Elo) +0.049 at 12 hours, +0.076 at the close; cross-league Elo +0.164 / +0.187. Our weight beside the market's in a joint fit: 0.01 (−0.18 to +0.17). Map 1: FORGE +0.004 (−0.007 to +0.015), other leagues +0.037. No metric was changed.
- **Fetching.** Kalshi refuses bursts: 8 and then 3 parallel requests spent most of two runs in backoff (the first run was killed at its 30-minute limit, a later one at 2 hours). It now fetches one contract per match, one request at a time with a 0.1 s pause, and goes straight to the historical endpoint for markets settled before Kalshi's cutoff. Network errors are retried.
- Docs: current_state (Working, Known limits, Future work: market follow-ups replace the plan), tech.md (pipeline, Predictions section, tests), product.md, README (Predictions), AGENTS.md (command, layout).
- Verification: `uv run pytest -q tests/test_evaluate_markets.py` (9 tests, synthetic, no network); full runs of the script against the live API and the built DB; the report numbers above are from the last run. `uv run pytest -q`: 122 passed.
- Limits: our calls are reconstructed and the forecast weights were fit on data that includes them; prices at the moment a call was saved aren't recorded yet; 52 Kalshi names unmatched; six series dropped where Kalshi's result disagreed with our games.

Covered leagues match fully (LCK, LEC, LCS, LPL except 3 teams, EM 116/126, CBLOL 14/15); misses are teams Oracle's Elixir lacks (CELOL, Komplettligaen, LCP Wildcard, Game Changers, Benelux, national teams).

Home: *Coming up*, the next 10 major-league and international matches within four days, under the FORGE head to head. `predictions.js`: local times, days regrouped by local date with Today/Tomorrow/Yesterday, started matches marked, filters. `predictions.json` publishes the matched log. Phones: each match becomes two lines inside the same table. The deploy role already has read-write on that bucket (checked in project-platform-infrastructure).

## 2026-10-02 — Tighter AURA rule; held-out combined test; team-centred late change adopted

- **Rule** (AGENTS.md, tech.md). An AURA change is compared with weights fit on earlier years only and tuning constants fixed in advance. It needs a significant gain in new-team r, or in split-half r with teammate r rising no more than 0.05; it must beat its team-only control (the added part replaced by the team's average in that game); split-half, new-team and roster r must not get significantly worse; ECE under 0.01 for snapshot-only models (end-of-game features aren't judged on calibration).
- **Held-out combined test** (scratch `$CLAUDE_JOB_DIR/tmp/combined/combined.py`; weights from earlier years, w = 0.25 fixed; major-league seasons from 2017: 2,031 player-seasons, 580 movers, 381 team-seasons). 15 min alone: split-half 0.590, teammates 0.266, new team 0.441, roster 0.642. S = 15 + ¼ team-centred change 15→25: split-half +0.026 (+0.017 to +0.033), new team +0.028 (+0.012 to +0.043), roster +0.001, teammates +0.015; its team-only control is the 15-minute term (the change sums to 0 per team). Passes. V = 15 + vision/frontline gaps: split-half +0.091 but teammates +0.080 and equal to its team-only control (−0.000, −0.025 to +0.024); new team +0.011 (n.s.; vs control +0.065, −0.007 to +0.132). Fails. SV (both): vs control split-half +0.008 (n.s.), teammates +0.080. Fails. ECE out of year: 15 min 0.0086, 25 min 0.0069.
- **Adopted S** in `prometheus/aura.py`: `get_aura` = 15-minute term + `LATE_WEIGHT` (0.25) × `team_centred` change to `LATE_MINUTES` (25, else 20); new `snapshot_scores`, `earlier_only`, `MIN_TRAIN_GAMES`. `evaluate_aura.py`: AURA is now the chosen score (snapshot terms alone are comparisons), new held-out section (`held_out`), `split_halves`/`season_pairs` take a score list, `roster_table` a score. In-year report: split-half 0.59, teammates 0.24, new team 0.48 (vs 15 min alone +0.018, +0.001 to +0.035); roster test unchanged (0.65; the late part sums to 0 per team).
- Docs: AGENTS.md, tech.md, current_state.md (Working, Known limits, Future work (b)/(c)), product.md, README.
- Verification: `evaluate_aura.py --out docs/aura_report.md` (44s; held-out numbers match the scratch run); `uv run pytest -q` passed 87 (new: earlier-only fits, the late part summing to 0 and keeping the team total, `team_centred`).
- Limit: the quarter weight came from the in-year experiment before being fixed for the held-out run, so the held-out test confirms it but didn't choose it blind. AURA still has no season aggregate or page.

## 2026-10-02 — AURA experiments: snapshot blends, share-split swing, full-game gaps, role-specific stats (nothing adopted yet)

Four scratch harnesses (subagents; `$CLAUDE_JOB_DIR/tmp/exp1`–`exp4`, not committed), each against AURA at 15 min on the same units (2,402 player-seasons split-half, 711 movers / 717 stayers, 452 team-seasons roster). Baseline reproduced in all four: split-half 0.550, teammate r 0.21, new team 0.458, roster 0.648. Δ = candidate − baseline, paired bootstrap 95%.

- **Snapshot blends.** Mean of 10/15/20/25 terms: split-half +0.089 (+0.073 to +0.104), new team +0.007 (−0.020 to +0.032), roster +0.048 (+0.036 to +0.061), but teammate r 0.37. 15 + w × team-centred change 15→25 (player's change minus teammates' mean): w 0.25 split-half +0.037 (+0.027 to +0.046), new team +0.018 (+0.001 to +0.035), teammate r 0.24, roster unchanged by construction; w 0.5 +0.057 / +0.017 (−0.015 to +0.048); w 1 +0.052 / −0.021. w picked on the same data. Mean of 10 and 15, and the 15→20 change: no gain (roster −0.015 for the former).
- **Share-split swing** (team's result − 15-min win chance, split by damage, gold, kill-participation, tower-damage or survival shares). Proportional and signed splits: split-half about +0.06, roster +0.07, new team +0.004 to +0.017 (n.s.), teammate r 0.45–0.49. An even split that ignores shares gains the same (+0.053, roster +0.072), so the gain is the team's result, not the shares. Share-only deviations: −0.021 to +0.009 split-half, new team flat. Rejected.
- **Full-game lane gaps as features.** All gaps (vision, wards, damage taken/mitigated, dpm, cspm, earned gpm, control wards): split-half +0.165, roster +0.090, new team −0.050 (−0.117 to +0.019), teammate r 0.68: mostly the team's result. Vision and frontline only (vspm, wcpm, damage taken, damage mitigated): split-half +0.102 (+0.068 to +0.136), new team +0.013 (−0.063 to +0.087), roster +0.011 (n.s.), teammate r 0.33. Candidate.
- **Role-specific stat sets.** Hand-picked per role (e.g. bot: gold/CS@15, damage share, gold share, kills): new team −0.291 (−0.431 to −0.146), worst for bot (0.43 → 0.16) and top (0.44 → 0.23); snapshot+shares only −0.098. Same full pool for every role, ridge or L1: split-half +0.16, roster +0.07–0.09, new team −0.074 to −0.084 (significant), teammate r 0.72–0.74. Rejected: within-team shares measure the role a team gives a player, not their skill, and don't travel.
- **Rule gap.** Several variants pass the rule as worded while mostly restating the team (teammate r tripling). The rule needs a teammate-overlap condition and a team-only control (like the even split).
- Data note: `player_stats` has 60 duplicate (gameid, teamid, position) keys; `load_aura_games` already drops those team-games (not five rows).

## 2026-10-02 — AURA benchmarks: roster test, substitution test, change rule

- **Roster test** (`roster_table` in `scripts/evaluate_aura.py`). Each starter's AURA from one half of a team-season (shrunk as if 5 average games were added), summed over the lineups used in the other half, against that half's win %; 452 major-league team-seasons, 10+ games per half, both directions pooled. Roster AURA r 0.65; team's own AURA 0.66 (roster − team −0.008, −0.016 to +0.001); GLORY 0.71 (−0.058, −0.087 to −0.030); win % 0.71 (−0.065, −0.095 to −0.037). Crediting players loses nothing against the team's own lane sum, but 15-minute lanes miss part of what GLORY sees.
- **Substitution test** (`substitutions`, `so_far`). 12,969 games where exactly one starter changed (every league). Incoming minus outgoing AURA so far (last 50 games, shrunk) against result minus Player Elo's expectation: r −0.006 (−0.023 to +0.012); both players 10+ games +0.008 (−0.015 to +0.030, 7,580); majors +0.022 (−0.020 to +0.064, 2,167). The lane gold gap is the same. Check that the test has power: over all 90,028 games the teams' summed AURA so far correlates 0.29 with the result (`pregame_gap_r`), but in swap games the AURA difference doesn't predict even the raw result (−0.011), so selection of substitutes dominates. Mean surprise in swap games −0.023 (teams do worse than Player Elo expects right after a swap).
- **Rule.** AGENTS.md and tech.md: an AURA change ships only on a significant paired-bootstrap gain in split-half reliability or new-team next-season r, neither significantly worse, roster test not significantly worse, ECE under 0.01; the substitution test is reported, not decisive; once AURA feeds a forecast, the forecast rule applies.
- Docs: current_state.md (Working, Known limits, Future work (c) and (d)), README, tech.md (AURA benchmarks, tests), AGENTS.md (rule, report time ~40s).
- Verification: `evaluate_aura.py --out docs/aura_report.md` (about 40s); a scratch check that AURA so far predicts results in ordinary games (team gap r 0.285, majors 0.252), now in the report; new `tests/test_evaluate_aura.py` (2 tests); `uv run pytest -q` passed 84.
- Limit: AURA's weights are fit on the whole year, so the substitution test's "so far" scores carry a small look-ahead in the weights (not in the player's games). The roster test's GLORY comparison needs `evaluate_season_stats.py` importable from `scripts/` (it is when run as a script; otherwise GLORY is skipped).

## 2026-10-02 — AURA step 1: calibrated snapshot model and per-player lane shares

- **Ingest fix** (`001_create_tables.sql`, `002_add_matches.py`). `player_stats` required every snapshot through 25 minutes, so it kept only 4,275 of 18,197 team-games under 25 minutes (mostly stomps). The 20- and 25-minute columns are now nullable; only Oracle's Elixir "partial" games (no snapshots) are dropped. Rows 846,036 → 909,338; short team-games with all five starters 4,275 → 16,307. `playerid` falls back as in `match_players`. GLORY, Elo and the forecasts don't read `player_stats`: `evaluate_metrics.py` and `evaluate_season_stats.py` reports are identical to before the rebuild, and `--check-weights` shows no change.
- **Model** (`prometheus/aura.py`, replacing `win_prediction.py` and `players.py`, which read a `player_raw_stats` table that no longer exists, and `AURA_NUMERIC_BASE_FEATURES`). Logistic regression of blue's win on the five lanes' gaps to their lane opponent (gold, XP, CS, kills, deaths, assists; 30 weights), fit per year. A player's AURA is their lane's log-odds term; the lane pair's scores cancel exactly.
- **Calibration** (`scripts/evaluate_aura.py`, `docs/aura_report.md`; fit on earlier years, scored on 2022–2027, about 46,000 games). Log loss at 10/15/20/25 min: 0.571 / 0.507 / 0.429 / 0.346 (blue-side baseline 0.691); ECE 0.007–0.008. An XGBoost model on the same team-level features (scratch harness) was worse at every snapshot (log loss +0.003 to +0.005, ECE 0.010–0.012), so the additive model, which splits into player shares exactly, costs nothing.
- **Which snapshot** (major-league player-seasons; 2,402 for split-half, 711 player pairs who changed team between consecutive seasons). Split-half r: 10 min 0.48, 15 min 0.55, 20 min 0.61, 25 min 0.65, lane gold gap at 15 0.54, team win % 0.65. Correlation with teammates in the same game: 0.08 / 0.21 / 0.35 / 0.56 (gold gap 0.40). Next season on a new team: 0.36 / 0.46 / 0.44 / 0.42, gold gap 0.42, win % 0.27 (same team: 0.50–0.60). 15 minutes chosen: against the gold gap, split-half +0.008 (−0.012 to +0.029), new team +0.043 (+0.005 to +0.081); against win % on a new team +0.189 (+0.104 to +0.277); against 25 min, new team +0.040 (−0.010 to +0.093, not significant) but far less team overlap.
- `half_of` moved from `evaluate_season_stats.py` to `prometheus/evaluation.py`; new `evaluation.paired_correlation_bootstrap`.
- Docs: current_state.md (Working, Known limits, Future work: AURA next steps and the new champion-matchup item; Cleanup note on the notebook), product.md, tech.md (tables, AURA, tests), AGENTS.md (layout, commands), README.
- Verification: `uv run bash scripts/setup_db.sh` (42s); `evaluate_aura.py --out docs/aura_report.md` (about 20s); `evaluate_metrics.py` and `evaluate_season_stats.py` diffed against the committed reports (identical); `uv run pytest -q` passed 82. New `tests/test_aura.py` (6 tests on synthetic data).
- Limit: AURA credits a lane only (jungle and support score against their counterpart; kills and assists aren't shared), and the deaths weights are positive once gold is in the model. No season aggregate or page yet. `notebooks/win_prediction.ipynb` still imports the removed module; `xgboost` stays in the dependencies. New files follow the repo's long-line style, as the existing modules do, rather than `black`'s default.

## 2026-10-02 — GlorELO+ renamed FORGE; Form sunset; Teams and Players menus; forecast-led home

- **Which stats earn a page** (from `docs/metric_backtest.md` and `docs/season_stats_report.md`, no new fits). Form alone vs Elo: domestic log loss +0.0015 (−0.0010 to +0.0040), international +0.0451 (+0.0301 to +0.0610). It doesn't beat Elo, so its page is sunset (`sunset`/`sunset_why` on its `FORECASTS` entry; page kept and rebuilt, listed on Sunset stats). Each lists its stats by name with a `question` (new field on every live metric). Teams ends with Sunset stats. `NAV`/`SECTIONS` are now flat per entity (no Season stats / Forecasts groups). ## 2026-10-02 — Player-built Elo (version 1, no synergy) replaces team Elo

- **Data.** New table `match_players` (001 schema, filled by `002_add_matches.py`): every team's five starters per game, 1,012,370 rows, every team-game has exactly five. `playerid` falls back to `name:<playername>|<teamid>` on 0.7% of rows.
- **Model** (`elo.compute_elo_records(..., rosters=)`, `load_rosters`, `load_elo_games`). Team rating = mean of the five starters' ratings + home-league offset; after a game every starter moves by the team's change (K=20, same game-length curve and league offsets). New player = average of the league's active players (last game there within `ACTIVE_DAYS` = 365), as the user chose. Players changing league keep their rating. Without rosters a team plays as one "player", which is plain team Elo except that a new team starts at its league's active average instead of 1500 + offset.
- **Benchmark** (scratch harness first, then `evaluate_metrics.py`, which now carries "Team Elo, no player ratings (live)" and a roster-slice table). Domestic log loss: Elo (live) 0.6319 vs team Elo 0.6343, −0.0024 (−0.0045 to −0.0003); international 0.6295 vs 0.6299, −0.0004 (−0.0064 to +0.0053). Passes the significance rule. Slices: first 10 games of a season −0.018 (−0.043 to +0.006, 324 games); within 10 games of a starter change −0.0012 (−0.0040 to +0.0017, 10,300 games; subs make this slice broad). Harness alternative "new player at 1500 + offset": domestic −0.0023, international +0.0017 (worse, not significant).
- **Knock-on.** GlorELO+ 0.6319 → 0.6291 domestic (harness, paired vs old GlorELO+: −0.0029, −0.0041 to −0.0017), international unchanged. Elo (as of cutoff) 0.6409 → 0.6393. Form uses pre-game Elo in its opponent adjustment: 0.6332 → 0.6335 (not significant; Form vs Elo is now +0.0015, −0.0010 to +0.0040). Weights refreshed with `--write-weights` (elo_weight 0.00317 → 0.00486, form_weight 0.651 → 0.501, cross-region 0.00842 → 0.00873); `--check-weights` clean afterwards.
- **Site and docs.** Elo explainer, team-page Elo note and figure caption, GlorELO+ accuracy (64.7%), README, product.md, current_state.md (Future work: player Elo follow-ups, synergy next), tech.md (Elo, tables, backtest).
- Verification: rebuilt the DB (`uv run bash scripts/setup_db.sh`; plain `bash` fails because `setup_db.sh` calls `python3` outside the uv env and the DB is deleted first). `evaluate_metrics.py --write-weights --out docs/metric_backtest.md`, `--check-weights`, `evaluate_season_stats.py --out docs/season_stats_report.md`. `uv run pytest -q` passed 63 (new: stable rosters reproduce team Elo, a transfer carries a player's rating, new players use only active players; the new-team test now expects the league's active average). `build_site.py` clean; `prometheus rankings glorelo+` ran; headless Chromium at 1440px and 390px on Elo, GlorELO+ and T1's page, no page errors or horizontal scroll.
- Limit: a team's published rating is its rating after its last game, so an off-season roster change shows only after its next game. No player ratings are published yet.

## 2026-10-01 — GLORY weights from half splits tested (not adopted); GLORY vs win %

- **Half-split weights.** Scratch harness (not committed). For each season, ridge regression (RidgeCV, alpha 0.01–1000) of one half's win % on the other half's Record-adjusted, per-year standardized stat averages, both directions, every *other* season only; team-seasons with 5+ games per half. Against current GLORY on the season-stat report's 547 team-seasons (paired bootstrap, 2,000 resamples): r with other half's win % 0.700 vs 0.697, +0.002 (−0.005 to +0.010); reliability 0.831 vs 0.828, +0.003 (−0.006 to +0.012). Not significant, so GLORY is unchanged. The weights did move as expected: turrets per 10 fell from the largest weight (0.30 per SD per game) to about level with gold per minute (0.08 and 0.07), and kills, dragons and first baron gained.
- **Full-season weights** (season averages → same-season win %, out of year): other half −0.001 (−0.007 to +0.005), reliability −0.012 (−0.019 to −0.005). Worse, and its weights lean even more on turrets (0.20), as the shared-games argument predicted.
- **GLORY vs win %.** Over a half season (about 37 games), both predict the other half's win % at 0.70 (difference +0.003, −0.018 to +0.024), but GLORY is far more stable (split-half 0.83 vs 0.70, +0.13). On a team's first k games of a half, predicting the other half's win % (460 team-seasons with 20+ games per half): 3 games GLORY 0.38 vs win % 0.33 (+0.05, +0.02 to +0.09); 5 games 0.47 vs 0.41 (+0.06); 10 games 0.56 vs 0.52 (+0.04, +0.01 to +0.07); 20 games 0.63 vs 0.63 (+0.00). GLORY's advantage is early in a season; by about 20 games win % has caught up.
- Verification: harness runs only; no code changed. `docs/steering/current_state.md` Future work updated.
- Limit: the few-games bootstrap resamples both halves of a team-season as separate rows, so those intervals are slightly too narrow.
- **Elo on the same question** (in game order: a team's first k major-league games of a season, predicting its win % from game 21 on; 486 team-seasons with 40+ games; correlations centred within league-season, because win % has no region strength and Elo does). Elo before game k+1 / win % / unadjusted GLORY / Record-adjusted GLORY: k=0 Elo 0.49; k=3 0.52 / 0.29 / 0.34 / 0.40; k=5 0.54 / 0.39 / 0.47 / 0.49; k=10 0.57 / 0.47 / 0.51 / 0.53; k=20 0.63 / 0.55 / 0.60 / 0.61. Elo minus unadjusted GLORY: +0.18 (+0.09 to +0.28) at 3 games, +0.03 (−0.01 to +0.07) at 20. Elo leads mostly through carry-over: its preseason rating alone beats 10 games of win %. Centring by year only, Elo looked worst at 20 games (0.49), an artefact of the region gap.
- Limit: the season-stat report and the GLORY vs win % numbers above centre by season only, not league-season, which slightly penalises any stat that carries region strength.

## 2026-10-01 — Early-game diffs tested for GLORY and Form (not adopted); significance rule

- **Question.** Neither GLORY nor Form used Oracle's Elixir's team gold, XP, CS or kill differences at 10, 15 or 20 minutes. The team rows carry them (`golddiffat15` etc.), but the LPL has none from 2022 on, and other leagues miss 2–20% of games. Tested in a scratch harness (not committed) that read them from the raw CSVs. Missing values were imputed per season by regression on the same game's GLORY stats, or set to 0.
- **GLORY** (same halves as the season-stat report, paired bootstrap over 547 team-seasons, 1,000 resamples). Every set made it slightly worse. Gold diff at 15: reliability −0.004 (−0.006 to −0.002), r with other half's win % −0.003 (−0.005 to −0.002). Gold/XP/CS at 15: −0.005 / −0.004. Every diff at 10 and 15: −0.005 / −0.004. Also adding 20: −0.007 / −0.006. Zero imputation was no better. On their own (opponent-adjusted team-season averages, LPL 2022+ missing), the diffs predict the other half's win % less well than gold per minute (0.69) or turrets (0.70): gold diff at 15 0.62, at 20 0.67. GLORY's end-of-game rates already carry what they hold.
- **Form** (backtest frame, Form weights fit out of year, domestic log loss, paired bootstrap). Adding gold diff at 15, gold at 10+15, gold/XP/CS at 15, all at 10+15, or all at 10/15/20 moved Form by −0.0001 to +0.0002 and GlorELO+ by −0.0001 to +0.0001. Every interval included 0, with or without imputation and with LPL games left out. Early diffs alone in place of GLORY's stats: Form +0.019 worse, GlorELO+ +0.002 worse.
- **Decision.** No metric changed. New rule (user's instruction): a metric changes only when its benchmark improves significantly over the current version. Recorded in `AGENTS.md` (Conventions), `tech.md` and `product.md`.
- **Season-stat report.** New column, "r with other half's win %": a stat on one half against win % on the other half, averaged over both directions. It gives an out-of-sample check that a season stat can't pass just by restating results. GLORY 0.70, unadjusted GLORY 0.72, old GLORY+ 0.67, GLORB 0.69, win % 0.70, Luck 0.05. `docs/season_stats_report.md` regenerated.
- **Future work.** Removed snapshot stats from Form follow-ups. Added "GLORY weights": GLORY's per-game weights do no better out of sample than turrets per 10 alone or win %, so weights fit at the team-season level may help.
- Verification: `uv run python scripts/evaluate_season_stats.py --out docs/season_stats_report.md` ran; `uv run pytest -q` passed 61.
- Limit: the paired comparisons for season stats live in the scratch harness, not in `evaluate_season_stats.py`; Record's "other half" value uses its own halves (every league), so it is not strictly comparable.

## 2026-10-01 — Season stats vs forecasts: GLORY absorbs GLORY+, Record, Luck, Form, new GlorELO+

- **Rule.** Season stats describe a team-season with hindsight and are judged by a new report; forecasts use only earlier games and are judged by the backtest; no forecast takes a season stat as input. Recorded in `current_state.md`, `tech.md`, `product.md`, `README.md`, `AGENTS.md`.
- **Season-stat report** (`scripts/evaluate_season_stats.py`, `docs/season_stats_report.md`; helpers `spearman_brown`, `games_for_reliability`, `correlation_interval` in `evaluation.py`). Random halves by game id. Full-season reliability / games to 0.5: win % 0.82 / 16, GLORY (Record-adjusted) 0.91 / 8, unadjusted GLORY 0.87 / 11, old GLORY+ 0.91 / 7, GLORB 0.86 / 12, Record 0.89 / 10, Luck 0.34 / 140.
- **Record and Luck** (`prometheus/season.py`). Record: per-season Bradley-Terry on game-length soft results over every league (ridge prior SD 1 log-odds), centred on the average major-league team, shown as % chance to beat it. Luck: win % minus earned win % from GLORY's per-game model. The first version (raw mean prediction) made strong teams look lucky (reliability 0.44, r with win % 0.59); a per-season, games-weighted calibration of earned on actual fixed most of it (0.34, 0.23). `elo.winner_score` was extracted from the Elo change so both use the same game-length curve.
- **GLORY absorbs GLORY+.** `get_glory_ranking(opponent_adjusted="record", record=...)` adjusts stats by each opponent's full-season Record (`adjust_for_opponent_record`) instead of pre-game Elo. Correlation with old GLORY+ 0.947, with Record 0.97. Unadjusted GLORY is a sunset page (`glory_unadjusted.html`); `glory_plus.html` redirects to GLORY (`redirect.html.j2`); the sunset page lists folded metrics with a link to their replacement.
- **Form** (`prometheus/form.py`): opponent-adjusted stats (pre-game Elo, coefficients from earlier seasons), exponentially weighted per team (half-life 20 games, carry 0.5 into a new season, prior 5 games of the season's running average), logistic weights on pre-game state gaps (`form_weights.json`), league-relative (minus the home league's season mean of pre-game scores). Home league = most-played domestic league this season (the last domestic league put most LPL teams in the Demacia Cup at year end and broke season joins).
- **Tuning (scratch harness, not committed).** On the backtest's games: raw stats Form alone was 0.6356 domestic / 0.6678 international; opponent adjustment brought it to 0.6324 / 0.6373. Grid (half-life 10/20/40 × carry 0.25/0.5/0.75 × prior 2/5/10): prior barely matters; 20/0.5/5 kept as a middle choice. Blends tried: 12 stat gaps + Elo (international 0.6331), one Form score + Elo (0.6323), cross-region interactions (0.6324), short + long memory Form (no gain), separate international fits (worse, too few games). Every Form-in-international variant lost to Elo alone (0.6297), so GlorELO+ uses Elo + league-relative Form within a league and Elo alone between leagues.
- **GlorELO+** (`glorelo.py`): rating = Elo + Form in Elo points (`form_weight / elo_weight`); same-league odds from the rating gap, cross-league from Elo on its own curve. `forecast.js` reads both weights from the page config and notes when it fell back to Elo. Backtest (`evaluate_metrics.py`, now forecasts only; Form weights fit out of year): domestic GlorELO+ 64.6%, log loss 0.6319 vs Elo live 0.6345 (−0.0026, −0.0037 to −0.0015); old GlorELO+ (GLORY+ + live Elo) was 0.6327 in the harness. International = Elo (0.6297; old blend 0.6290, difference not significant). Weights written with `--write-weights`; `--check-weights` now warns only on the three blend weights (Form stat weights near zero made relative changes noisy). The backtest dropped from about 22 to 8 seconds without per-cutoff GLORY fits.
- **Site.** Header and contents: Season stats (GLORY, Record, Luck), Forecasts (GlorELO+, Form, Elo), Sunset stats. New pages `record.html`, `luck.html` (fixed signed scale via config `domain`), `form.html` (every league, now and season-end rows), plus the sunset pages above. Record lists the same team-seasons as GLORY with GLORY's league (Record's own home league left out 29 promoted or academy team-seasons). Team pages: GLORY/Record figure switch; register GLORY, year rank, Record, Luck, season-end GlorELO+. Captions name each metric (`value_word`/`value_plural`). Build takes about 10 seconds (Form pass about 3).
- **CLI.** `prometheus rankings glory|record|luck|glorelo+|form|glorb`; `glory+` removed (it was added earlier today and is now part of GLORY).
- Verification: `uv run pytest -q` passed 61 (new `test_season.py`, `test_form.py`, rewritten `test_glorelo.py`, record-adjustment and reliability tests), including e2e on the real DB. Ran `evaluate_metrics.py --write-weights --out docs/metric_backtest.md`, `--check-weights` (no warning), and `evaluate_season_stats.py --out docs/season_stats_report.md`. CLI smoke runs for every metric (with year ranges, league filters). Clean `output/teams` rebuild; headless Chromium at 1440px and 390px, no page errors: every rankings page, sunset, home, T1's team page (register fits at 390px), head to head Gen.G v T1 (68 in 100, same league) and Gen.G v Bilibili (61 in 100, Elo note shown), Form 2019 LPL (18 teams, FPX first), `glory_plus.html` redirect. Impeccable detector: advisories only.
- Limit: Form's hyperparameters were chosen on the backtest that reports them; series odds are not built yet; the header no longer fits on one line, so the search sits on its own row on desktop; see Known limits in `current_state.md`.

Rendering new team HTML with the previous `register.css` reproduces it: both the GLORY and GLORY+ labels print over each bar, because the old CSS only hides `.season-val--glorb`. Fix: templates link CSS and JS through `asset()`, which adds a content-hash `?v=`, so a changed file always has a new URL (browser caches as well as CloudFront).

The header search is now "Team · Go to a team": `search.js` fetches `teams.json` (all 2,087 teams; name, slug, league, last game; major-league teams first, then newest; 148KB, about 30KB gzipped) on first focus and suggests up to eight matches (exact, prefix, word start, anywhere). Arrow keys and Enter or a click open the team page. With no match, or without JS, the form submits to the Elo register search as before. The label is "Team" so the head stays on one line down to 1280px.

- **Backtest.** "Elo (as of cutoff)" now adds league-offset moves since each team's last game (`evaluation.elo_as_of`), matching the published rating. Domestic log loss 0.6410 → 0.6409, international 0.6405 → 0.6402; GlorELO+ vs Elo deltas moved by ≤0.0002. Weights are fit on live Elo, so `--write-weights` left `glorelo_weights.json` unchanged.

## 2026-10-01 — Forecast pages open on now; seasons show year-end ratings

- Elo and GlorELO+ pages open on current ratings: Elo for teams that played in the last 183 days (`ACTIVE_WINDOW`), GlorELO+ for this season. Before, the Elo page listed every team ever at its last rating, so SK Telecom T1 (last active 2019) topped it.
- The year picker is now "season": picking seasons switches to one row per team-season, rated at the end of that calendar year (new `elo.get_season_elos`, one query plus pandas, about 1.3s). GlorELO+ gets past seasons too (that season's GLORY+ with year-end Elo). Head to head stays on today's ratings. Search on the Elo page still reaches every team's current rating, including retired teams, since the header search lands there.
- UI: picker empty value *Now*, a "Current ratings" text button in its panel, a Season column and caption wording for season views; Elo and GlorELO+ margin notes rewritten (including the league-offset Elo note). DESIGN.md filter-line spec, tech.md and current_state.md updated.
- Verification: `uv run pytest -q` passed, including a new integration test for season-end ratings with offset changes. Built the site and checked Elo (now, 2019, picker open) and GlorELO+ (now, 2019+2024) at 1440px and 390px in headless Chromium: no page errors, captions and counts correct, 2019 led by FunPlus Phoenix; searches for retired teams return them.
- Limit: GlorELO+ season ratings are centred per season, so cross-season comparisons are relative to each season's average.

## 2026-10-01 — Elo league offsets (international games move the whole region)

- `compute_elo_records`: rating = own rating + home league offset. A cross-league international game also moves each league's offset by `LEAGUE_SHARE` (0.5) of the team's change. New teams start at 1500 + their league's offset. `get_latest_elos` adds offset changes since a team's last game. Schema: `home_league` and `league_offset` columns on `game_length_elo`, plus a new `game_length_elo_league_offsets` table.
- Tuned with a scratch harness that replays Elo on the backtest's games (not committed). Kept: league share 0.5 (league_k 10 at K=20). Rejected: changing K (25–30 helps domestic by about 0.0005 but hurts international), a between-season pull to the league mean (hurts international by 0.002–0.01), an international K multiplier, and a "most common of the last 20 games" home-league rule (no measurable difference).
- Backtest: international Elo log loss 0.6540 → 0.6405 as of cutoff, 0.6456 → 0.6297 live (accuracy 60.6% → 65.0%); domestic 0.6414 → 0.6410 and 0.6349 → 0.6345. GlorELO+ weights refreshed with `--write-weights` (0.00983 / 0.00634).
- Verification: rebuilt the DB; `evaluate_metrics.py --write-weights --out docs/metric_backtest.md`; `build_site.py` ran. New unit and integration tests for offsets, new-team starts and latest-rating refresh pass. `pytest -q` passed 43 after the rebuild. Docs (current_state, tech, product, README) updated in a follow-up commit; the site was checked in the forecast-page entry above.
- Limit: the backtest's "as of cutoff" Elo uses each team's post-game rating without later offset changes; team-page Elo history is also per game.

## 2026-10-01 — GlorELO+ weights in a tracked file, checked in CI

- The weights moved from constants in `prometheus/glorelo.py` to `prometheus/glorelo_weights.json` (package data in `pyproject.toml`). `glorelo.py` loads them and adds `load_weights`, `save_weights` and `weight_changes`.
- `evaluate_metrics.py --write-weights` saves the refit weights; `--check-weights` refits without scoring the report and prints a `::warning::` annotation when a weight moves more than 10% (`WEIGHT_TOLERANCE`). Both refuse `--years`, since the weights are fit on every season. `publish.yml` runs the check after the tests.
- The backtest now takes about 22 seconds instead of about 90, a side effect of the Elo table key from the previous entry.
- Verification: `uv run pytest -q` passed (new round-trip and loading tests); `--check-weights` on the real DB reported +0.0% and +0.1%; a forced 23% move printed the warning; `--write-weights --out docs/metric_backtest.md` left both the weights file and the report unchanged.
- Limit: CI warns but does not commit refreshed weights; the warning has not yet been seen in a real Actions run.

## 2026-10-01 — Faster site builds (17s → 5s)

- Profiled the build: model fitting took about 0.03s; the time went to SQLite reads. `game_length_elo` had no key, so each of GLORY+'s 13 pre-game Elo joins built a temporary index (about 0.5s each), and the five ranking calls read every year's games twice per call.
- `game_length_elo` now has `PRIMARY KEY(gameid, teamid)` (`001_create_tables.sql`). `get_glory_ranking` reads each year's major-league games once and uses them for both the fit and the averages; new `load_glory_games()` and a `games=` argument let `build_site.py` share one read across its five calls. `regression.fit_glory_pipeline()` fits on a frame already in memory.
- Verification: rebuilt the DB with `setup_db.sh`; `build_site.py` went from 17.2s to 5.2s, and all 2,094 generated HTML files are byte-identical to the build before the change. `uv run pytest -q` passed, including a new test that shared and fresh reads give the same ranking.
- Limit: the backtest still reads per cutoff; it gets the one-read-per-year saving but no cross-call sharing.

- Renamed GlorELO to GlorELO+. New `prometheus/glorelo.py` turns this season's GLORY+ and each team's latest Elo into an Elo-scale rating (average major-league team = 1500) using weights from the backtest. New `glorelo_plus.html` with a head-to-head box (`site_static/js/forecast.js`).

## 2026-10-01 — GlorELO blend in the backtest (PR #7)

- Win curves accept several rating gaps, so a blend is a metric with two inputs. Scored GLORY+ + Elo, a games-played variant, and a live-Elo version, plus direct blend-vs-Elo comparisons.
- Results: beats Elo on domestic log loss (−0.0038, CI excludes 0); ties Elo internationally; the games-played variant adds nothing. GLORY+ and Elo gaps correlate at 0.83.
- Month-cluster bootstrap (run ad hoc, not yet in the report): GlorELO+'s domestic accuracy edge over Elo is +0.35 points (−0.08 to +0.80), not significant. GLORY+ beats GLORY internationally by +3.0 points (+0.6 to +5.5).
- Verification: `pytest -q` passed 32; full backtest run.

## 2026-10-01 — Metric backtest (PR #6)

- `scripts/evaluate_metrics.py` and `prometheus/evaluation.py`: rolling monthly backtest with a `before` cutoff added to `get_glory_ranking`; out-of-year logistic win curves; accuracy, Brier, log loss; paired bootstrap against win % so far. Domestic and cross-region international test sets.
- Results: GLORY barely beats win %, GLORB does not; GLORY+ is clearly better than GLORY internationally; Elo is best overall.
- Verification: `pytest -q` passed 30; full run takes about 90 seconds.
